## EAA-001 — Explicit agent–environment boundary

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-001

**Native name.** Explicit agent–environment boundary

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/0

**Original status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Selected status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Bearer and scope.** A described agent together with its environment, observations, action channels and task, under an explicitly selected definition of agency.

**Quantifier and horizon.** For each consequential agency attribution in this study; no claim that all schools share necessary and sufficient conditions.

**Applicability guard.** Agency terminology is used to explain or compare behavior.

**Material premises.** A task boundary can be stated without hiding a material human, tool or environmental dependency.

**Evaluation rule.** Recover what senses, selects and acts, what lies outside it, and which definition is being applied. A change of boundary requires the attribution to be reconsidered.

**Constructed positive case.** A thermostat is an agent under a situated feedback taxonomy while lacking the stronger mentalistic commitments of another account.

**Constructed failure witness.** Calling an entire service autonomous while its next action is selected by an undisclosed human makes the comparison indeterminate.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A small controller can be described directly by its input-output behavior; an agent label adds no obligatory machinery.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain the explicit boundary as an analytical requirement, not a universal definition. Wooldridge and Jennings distinguish weak and strong notions; Franklin and Graesser offer a selected taxonomy; Luck and d’Inverno formalize a different hierarchy. Their disagreement is substantive.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S001; EAA-S002; EAA-S003

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Competing weak/strong, situated-persistent and motivation-based definitions, not one necessary-and-sufficient academic consensus.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S001

**Date.** 1995-06

**Work.** Intelligent Agents: Theory and Practice

**Version.** Knowledge Engineering Review 10(2), 115–152; author HTML

**Source id.** EAA-S002

**Date.** 1997

**Work.** Is it an Agent, or just a Program?: A Taxonomy for Autonomous Agents

**Version.** ATAL 1996 workshop; LNCS 1193 proceedings pp.21–35; author HTML

**Source id.** EAA-S003

**Date.** 1995

**Work.** A Formal Framework for Agency and Autonomy

**Version.** ICMAS 1995 pp.254–260

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Mechanism.** Declare the entity, environmental variables it can observe or affect, objective attribution, persistence and the operative definition of agency. Keep interface boundaries distinct from metaphysical claims.

**Trigger or context.** Comparing systems, assigning responsibility or claiming autonomous capability.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** For a pure function, describe input/output computation without forcing an agent label. A simple controller may satisfy a minimal situated definition.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** An identifiable task and observation/action interface; no requirement for a human-like self-model.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Architect, evaluator and person interpreting capability claims

**Boundary.** This is a research/evaluation criterion or rejected inference; it does not itself confer operative authority. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. An identifiable task and observation/action interface; no requirement for a human-like self-model.

**Observable postcondition.** An evaluator can tell which changes are agent actions, environmental events, or actions of another actor.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** The definitions disagree: internally motivated autonomy is not identical to externally goal-directed situated persistence. A tidy boundary can still omit environmental dependencies.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C001

**Source ids.** EAA-S001; EAA-S003

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** The definitions disagree: internally motivated autonomy is not identical to externally goal-directed situated persistence. A tidy boundary can still omit environmental dependencies.; Introduced cost or harm: Modelling effort and risk of freezing an arbitrary boundary.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** The definitions disagree: internally motivated autonomy is not identical to externally goal-directed situated persistence. A tidy boundary can still omit environmental dependencies.

**Source ids.** EAA-S001; EAA-S003

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** Which definition is being used, and does the demonstrated system actually meet it?; Boundary adequacy is relative to the causal question; nested agents and environments can require different resolutions.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain an explicit operational boundary while preserving alternative definitions rather than declaring one universal essence.

**Condition.** Comparing systems, assigning responsibility or claiming autonomous capability.

**Limit.** The definitions disagree: internally motivated autonomy is not identical to externally goal-directed situated persistence. A tidy boundary can still omit environmental dependencies.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

<a id="eaa-002"></a>

## EAA-002 — Task-relative autonomy dimensions

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-002

**Native name.** Task-relative autonomy dimensions

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/1

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** A particular delegated system on a specified task and environment, including human intervention.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Compare configurations over the same task and operating period; dimensions need not form one scalar ranking.

**Applicability guard.** A decision depends on how much and what kind of autonomy a system has.

**Material premises.** Task, environment difficulty, support available and intervention policy are stated.

**Evaluation rule.** Describe the relevant independence, capability and interaction dimensions. Do not infer superior outcomes from a larger autonomy label.

**Constructed positive case.** A mobile robot navigates independently but needs human selection of destinations; a scheduler chooses times but cannot send invitations without delegated scope.

**Constructed failure witness.** A scalar autonomy score treats a difficult environment with occasional help as inferior to a trivial unattended task.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** For a deterministic batch job with fixed permissions, ordinary interface and responsibility descriptions can be adequate.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain as a practical comparison rule. ALFUS supplies task/environment/human-independence dimensions, but Luck and d’Inverno call autonomy absolute within their formal framework. Do not attribute consensus relativity to that school. The user study shows experienced control is not captured by marketing labels.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S003; EAA-S017; EAA-S047

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Contextual descriptions of agent autonomy, with ALFUS organising mission/environment/human-independence terminology.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S001

**Date.** 1995-06

**Work.** Intelligent Agents: Theory and Practice

**Version.** Knowledge Engineering Review 10(2), 115–152; author HTML

**Source id.** EAA-S003

**Date.** 1995

**Work.** A Formal Framework for Agency and Autonomy

**Version.** ICMAS 1995 pp.254–260

**Source id.** EAA-S017

**Date.** 2008-10

**Work.** Autonomy Levels for Unmanned Systems (ALFUS) Framework, Volume I: Terminology

**Version.** NIST Special Publication 1011-I-2.0, Version 2.0

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Mechanism.** Describe separately what the agent chooses, what resources and duration it controls, what it can learn, and the conditions under which people intervene; relate these to task/environment difficulty.

**Trigger or context.** Comparing or delegating different degrees of autonomy.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A short capability-and-permission description is enough when only one decision is delegated; no multi-axis scoring dashboard is obligatory.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** Explicit task context and independently described capability and authorisation.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Delegator and capability evaluator

**Boundary.** This is a research/evaluation criterion or rejected inference; it does not itself confer operative authority. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Explicit task context and independently described capability and authorisation.

**Observable postcondition.** Two systems with different goal, action and oversight powers are not treated as equivalent merely because both are called autonomous.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** ALFUS terminology is guidance, not a validated universal ranking; more independence may reduce useful human control.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C001

**Source ids.** EAA-S043; EAA-S047

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** ALFUS terminology is guidance, not a validated universal ranking; more independence may reduce useful human control.; Introduced cost or harm: Comparison overhead and false numerical precision.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** ALFUS terminology is guidance, not a validated universal ranking; more independence may reduce useful human control.

**Source ids.** EAA-S043; EAA-S047

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** Which dimensions are actually delegated, and what changes when the environment or task changes?; Dimensions are an analytical reconciliation; ALFUS does not itself prescribe every dimension listed here.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Use a task-relative vector or equivalent description, not a ladder whose highest level is automatically preferable.

**Condition.** Comparing or delegating different degrees of autonomy.

**Limit.** ALFUS terminology is guidance, not a validated universal ranking; more independence may reduce useful human control.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

## EAA-003 — Computational grounding of mental-state vocabulary

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-003

**Native name.** Computational grounding of mental-state vocabulary

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/2

**Original status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Selected status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Bearer and scope.** A computational architecture using belief, desire, intention or commitment as explanatory state.

**Quantifier and horizon.** For each mental-state term claimed to have operational or formal force; evaluated over the specified interpreter transitions.

**Applicability guard.** Mental-state vocabulary is offered as more than informal shorthand.

**Material premises.** The state representation, update rules and action-selection consequences can be identified.

**Evaluation rule.** Trace at least one state update and its consequences, and distinguish the implemented semantics from the stronger logic used to discuss it.

**Constructed positive case.** An AgentSpeak intention stack and event selection rule determine which plan step runs after a belief update.

**Constructed failure witness.** A prompt labeled intention neither persists a commitment nor changes action selection when the stated intention is contradicted.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A simple reflex controller needs no mental-state terminology; informal descriptions may still be useful without claiming BDI semantics.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. Shoham explicitly asks when intentional descriptions are useful; Rao and Georgeff distinguish abstract logical assumptions from implementable interpreters. Computational grounding does not entail consciousness or guarantee task success.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S009; EAA-S010; EAA-S028

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Mental-state programming and BDI logic with explicitly discussed abstraction/interpreter gaps.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S009

**Date.** 1995

**Work.** BDI Agents: From Theory to Practice

**Version.** ICMAS 1995 pp.312–319

**Source id.** EAA-S010

**Date.** 1993

**Work.** Agent-oriented programming

**Version.** Artificial Intelligence 60, 51–92; full journal scan

**Source id.** EAA-S028

**Date.** 2007

**Work.** On the Formal Semantics of Speech-Act Based Communication in an Agent-Oriented Programming Language

**Version.** JAIR 29, 221–267; arXiv upload 2011

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Mechanism.** Link each engineering use of belief, desire, intention or commitment to a representation, transition, consumer and observable behaviour; distinguish an explanatory stance from executable semantics.

**Trigger or context.** Claims of BDI, persistent intention, belief revision or communicative understanding.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use ordinary state, task and action names when intentional vocabulary adds no predictive or engineering value.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** Accessible implementation semantics or an explicitly scoped abstract specification.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Implementer, verifier and evaluator

**Boundary.** This is a research/evaluation criterion or rejected inference; it does not itself confer operative authority. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Accessible implementation semantics or an explicitly scoped abstract specification.

**Observable postcondition.** A change in the claimed mental state has a specified consequence, or the claim is marked descriptive rather than implemented.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Formal predicates may be infinitely closed while an interpreter is finite and asynchronous; prompts with the same words do not establish conformance.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C001

**Source ids.** EAA-S009; EAA-S010

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Formal predicates may be infinitely closed while an interpreter is finite and asynchronous; prompts with the same words do not establish conformance.; Introduced cost or harm: Specification and traceability effort; over-formalising a simple controller can be wasteful.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Formal predicates may be infinitely closed while an interpreter is finite and asynchronous; prompts with the same words do not establish conformance.

**Source ids.** EAA-S009; EAA-S010

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** What behaviour distinguishes having the named state from merely displaying its label?; This is not a test of consciousness or moral personhood, and those claims are outside the technical evidence.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain useful intentional abstractions but demand an explicit abstraction-to-implementation relation for technical claims.

**Condition.** Claims of BDI, persistent intention, belief revision or communicative understanding.

**Limit.** Formal predicates may be infinitely closed while an interpreter is finite and asynchronous; prompts with the same words do not establish conformance.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

<a id="eaa-004"></a>

## EAA-004 — Capability is not delegated permission

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-004

**Native name.** Capability is not delegated permission

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/3

**Original status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Selected status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Bearer and scope.** An agent’s proposed consequential action relative to the person or institution that delegates its scope.

**Quantifier and horizon.** For every action requiring delegated authority, at the time it is committed; not a requirement for repeated approval of already delegated actions.

**Applicability guard.** An available capability can affect resources, people or commitments beyond the agent’s own internal computation.

**Material premises.** The relevant authority and current delegation can be established at useful granularity.

**Evaluation rule.** A capability may be selected only inside the effective delegation; uncertainty about a material permission is not resolved by technical feasibility.

**Constructed positive case.** A calendar assistant reschedules within a user-approved window automatically and asks only when the proposed move exceeds that window.

**Constructed failure witness.** A tool can delete a shared folder and the agent treats the successful permission check of its service account as consent from affected collaborators.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** An already authorized, reversible routine step requires no new ceremony. A purely simulated action has no external delegation effect.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain as a normative and interface distinction supported by mixed-initiative design, interface-agent thresholds and user-autonomy criticism. These sources do not establish one universal legal rule or a mandatory approval button.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S019; EAA-S043; EAA-S044; EAA-S033

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Human control over adaptive assistants, including action-specific suggestion versus execution thresholds.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S019

**Date.** 1999

**Work.** Principles of Mixed-Initiative User Interfaces

**Version.** CHI 1999; author manuscript

**Source id.** EAA-S043

**Date.** 1997

**Work.** Software Agents and User Autonomy

**Version.** Proceedings of Autonomous Agents 1997, pp.466–469

**Source id.** EAA-S044

**Date.** 1993

**Work.** Learning Interface Agents

**Version.** AAAI 1993, pp.459–465

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Mechanism.** Keep the means the system can execute separate from what the delegator has authorised; make consequential scope boundaries usable at action selection and dispatch.

**Trigger or context.** Actions affect other people, user resources, external accounts, physical systems or protected information.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** No additional permission mechanism is needed for an already constrained, harmless action entirely within the declared task; a fixed capability sandbox may suffice.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** A source of task authority, an interpretable scope and an actuator that can respect limitations.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Delegator, executor and affected participant

**Boundary.** This is a research/evaluation criterion or rejected inference; it does not itself confer operative authority. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A source of task authority, an interpretable scope and an actuator that can respect limitations.

**Observable postcondition.** An out-of-scope action is refused or referred rather than legitimised by a high success probability.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Granular confirmations can overwhelm people; user permission alone may not settle rights or safety of affected third parties.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C007; EAA-C012

**Source ids.** EAA-S033; EAA-S043

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Granular confirmations can overwhelm people; user permission alone may not settle rights or safety of affected third parties.; Introduced cost or harm: Friction, delayed action and permission-management complexity.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Granular confirmations can overwhelm people; user permission alone may not settle rights or safety of affected third parties.

**Source ids.** EAA-S033; EAA-S043

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** What evidence establishes permission for this means, as distinct from capability and a broad task request?; Authority is a normative/task relation, not a learned affordance score. Specific legal obligations are not derived here.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Preserve meaningful control at consequential decisions, not maximally frequent consent prompts or blanket autonomy.

**Condition.** Actions affect other people, user resources, external accounts, physical systems or protected information.

**Limit.** Granular confirmations can overwhelm people; user permission alone may not settle rights or safety of affected third parties.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

**Requalification analysis.** **Property id.** EAA-004

**Native name.** Capability is not delegated permission

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/3

**Original status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Selected status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Bearer and scope.** An agent’s proposed consequential action relative to the person or institution that delegates its scope.

**Quantifier and horizon.** For every action requiring delegated authority, at the time it is committed; not a requirement for repeated approval of already delegated actions.

**Applicability guard.** An available capability can affect resources, people or commitments beyond the agent’s own internal computation.

**Material premises.** The relevant authority and current delegation can be established at useful granularity.

**Evaluation rule.** A capability may be selected only inside the effective delegation; uncertainty about a material permission is not resolved by technical feasibility.

**Constructed positive case.** A calendar assistant reschedules within a user-approved window automatically and asks only when the proposed move exceeds that window.

**Constructed failure witness.** A tool can delete a shared folder and the agent treats the successful permission check of its service account as consent from affected collaborators.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** An already authorized, reversible routine step requires no new ceremony. A purely simulated action has no external delegation effect.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain as a normative and interface distinction supported by mixed-initiative design, interface-agent thresholds and user-autonomy criticism. These sources do not establish one universal legal rule or a mandatory approval button.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S019; EAA-S043; EAA-S044; EAA-S033

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.


<a id="eaa-005"></a>

## EAA-005 — Observation, stored belief and world state remain distinct

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-005

**Native name.** Observation, stored belief and world state remain distinct

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/4

**Original status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Selected status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Bearer and scope.** An agent’s observations and stored beliefs about an external state.

**Quantifier and horizon.** At each inference from a received or remembered value to a world fact; over the observation’s validity interval.

**Applicability guard.** Observations can be noisy, delayed, incomplete, socially supplied or invalidated.

**Material premises.** Evidence origin and the update interpretation are available or their absence is disclosed.

**Evaluation rule.** Keep an observation, its interpretation, and the asserted world condition conceptually separable; revise beliefs when contrary material evidence appears.

**Constructed positive case.** A presence sensor reports no keyboard activity, but the scheduling agent treats availability as uncertain rather than asserting the user has left.

**Constructed failure witness.** A received statement is inserted as trusted fact, although the sender may be mistaken or the report stale.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A single atomic reading of a stable local register can justify a direct action; no probabilistic database is thereby required.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. POMDP beliefs are model-relative distributions, not the world itself; Electric Elves reports proxy failures; AgentSpeak source annotations and social acceptance are not truth certificates.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S012; EAA-S021; EAA-S028

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** An observation-conditioned state estimate; early office assistance initially inferred availability from a poor proxy.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S012

**Date.** 1998

**Work.** Planning and Acting in Partially Observable Stochastic Domains

**Version.** Artificial Intelligence 101, 99–134

**Source id.** EAA-S021

**Date.** undated author manuscript (workshop-era; exact year unverified)

**Work.** Electric Elves: What Went Wrong and Why

**Version.** Six-page author manuscript; distinct from the later 2008 AI Magazine article

**Source id.** EAA-S028

**Date.** 2007

**Work.** On the Formal Semantics of Speech-Act Based Communication in an Agent-Oriented Programming Language

**Version.** JAIR 29, 221–267; arXiv upload 2011

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Mechanism.** Retain the relevant observation source and interpretation; update a state estimate without equating observation, inference and reality. Gather discriminating evidence when the distinction changes action.

**Trigger or context.** Noisy sensors, tool output, inferred availability, delayed messages or conflicting reports.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use a directly validated signal without a separate belief database when ambiguity cannot affect the decision.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** An observation interface and a way to distinguish source reports from inferred state.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** State estimator and action selector

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. An observation interface and a way to distinguish source reports from inferred state.

**Observable postcondition.** Decision-relevant uncertainty or disagreement survives into the action decision instead of being silently erased.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** A POMDP represents uncertainty only under its model; adding probability does not repair an omitted observation failure.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C004

**Source ids.** EAA-S021

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** A POMDP represents uncertainty only under its model; adding probability does not repair an omitted observation failure.; Introduced cost or harm: Storage, reconciliation and the possibility of excessive uncertainty blocking useful action.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** A POMDP represents uncertainty only under its model; adding probability does not repair an omitted observation failure.

**Source ids.** EAA-S021

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** Which apparent facts are observations or inferences, and what contrary signal would revise them?; The necessary state granularity depends on action consequences and perceptual aliasing.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Preserve provenance and actionable uncertainty; prefer direct feedback where it supplies sufficient discrimination.

**Condition.** Noisy sensors, tool output, inferred availability, delayed messages or conflicting reports.

**Limit.** A POMDP represents uncertainty only under its model; adding probability does not repair an omitted observation failure.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

<a id="eaa-006"></a>

## EAA-006 — Decision-relevant freshness and change detection

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-006

**Native name.** Decision-relevant freshness and change detection

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/5

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** Decision-relevant environmental or control information used by an acting system.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Before the next consequential use, relative to the rate of change and consequence of error; no universal polling period.

**Applicability guard.** The underlying fact may have changed enough to alter the action choice or its admissibility.

**Material premises.** A useful change indicator, timestamp, atomicity boundary or conservative fallback exists.

**Evaluation rule.** Refresh or validate material assumptions before relying on them; bound the interval in which a decision can use obsolete information.

**Constructed positive case.** A resource allocation is checked again after another actor could have consumed it.

**Constructed failure witness.** A plan continues after a diagnosed actuator failure because its cached capability status is never invalidated.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** An immutable task specification or atomic transaction needs no repeated observation; refresh only information capable of changing the decision.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain conditionally. Tileworld demonstrates dependence on environmental dynamism and reconsideration cost; reactive execution and DS1 illustrate monitoring limits. These do not prescribe universal synchronous revalidation.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S015; EAA-S016; EAA-S022

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Reactive context checks and reconsideration in changing environments, alongside asynchronous executive state.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S015

**Date.** 1991

**Work.** Commitment and Effectiveness of Situated Agents

**Version.** IJCAI 1991 pp.82–88

**Source id.** EAA-S016

**Date.** 1987

**Work.** An Investigation into Reactive Planning in Complex Domains

**Version.** AAAI 1987 pp.202–206

**Source id.** EAA-S022

**Date.** 1999

**Work.** Validating the DS1 Remote Agent Experiment

**Version.** ISAIRAS 1999 primary experiment report

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Mechanism.** Associate consequential observations and decisions with their validity conditions; re-observe or invalidate them when elapsed time, events or concurrent actions can change preconditions.

**Trigger or context.** Dynamic environments, delayed tool responses, long deliberation or shared mutable objects.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Reuse a state estimate without refresh when the relevant variables are stable or the action is insensitive to change.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** A model of which changes matter, access to a current signal when needed and an actionable response to invalidation.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Executor and state-estimation process

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A model of which changes matter, access to a current signal when needed and an actionable response to invalidation.

**Observable postcondition.** An action whose supporting state is stale is rechecked, replaced or withheld before preventable misuse.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Polling everything makes observations and action slower; timestamp recency alone does not imply semantic validity.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C004

**Source ids.** EAA-S015; EAA-S022

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Polling everything makes observations and action slower; timestamp recency alone does not imply semantic validity.; Introduced cost or harm: Sensing latency, tool expense, race windows and false invalidation.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Polling everything makes observations and action slower; timestamp recency alone does not imply semantic validity.

**Source ids.** EAA-S015; EAA-S022

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** What can change between observation and effect, and how is that change handled?; No universal expiry interval follows from the literature; change rate and consequence determine usefulness.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Use selective event- or risk-triggered refresh rather than permanent global resensing.

**Condition.** Dynamic environments, delayed tool responses, long deliberation or shared mutable objects.

**Limit.** Polling everything makes observations and action slower; timestamp recency alone does not imply semantic validity.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

**Native name.** Decision-relevant freshness and change detection

<a id="eaa-007"></a>

## EAA-007 — Adequate uncertainty representation rather than a complete world model

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-007

**Native name.** Adequate uncertainty representation rather than a complete world model

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/6

**Original status.** ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE

**Selected status.** ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE

**Bearer and scope.** The information state used to choose actions in a partially observed task.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Adequacy is relative to the chosen objective, observation history and horizon, not reconstruction of every world feature.

**Applicability guard.** Different hidden states compatible with current observation require materially different actions.

**Material premises.** The uncertainty representation or memory preserves distinctions needed for the task; formal sufficiency additionally requires the stated transition and observation model.

**Evaluation rule.** Check whether histories collapsed into one representation can demand incompatible decisions. If they can, retain additional state or accept a bounded performance loss.

**Constructed positive case.** A controller remembers whether a door was previously unlocked when the current image alone cannot show lock status.

**Constructed failure witness.** Two hidden modes give the same sensor reading but opposite safe actions, and a memoryless controller must choose the same action in both.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** Fully observed or suitably structured tasks can use a small reactive state; some compiled POMDP controllers avoid an explicit online belief vector.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain as assumption-sensitive. POMDP belief sufficiency is a model theorem, while Brooks establishes a serious alternative to full symbolic modelling. Neither establishes universal representation necessity or sufficiency.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S005; EAA-S012; EAA-S021

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION; DOCUMENTATION_REPAIR

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Belief-state planning under a specified partial-observation model, contrasted with direct environmental coupling.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S005

**Date.** 1991

**Work.** Intelligence without representation

**Version.** Artificial Intelligence 47, 139–159; author reformatted copy

**Source id.** EAA-S012

**Date.** 1998

**Work.** Planning and Acting in Partially Observable Stochastic Domains

**Version.** Artificial Intelligence 101, 99–134

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE

**Mechanism.** Use history, a sufficient statistic, a belief distribution or a smaller task-relevant state estimate only to the extent needed for action discrimination.

**Trigger or context.** Partial observability, uncertain transitions or delayed effects make an observation-only policy inadequate.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A reactive mapping is sufficient when observations already determine an adequate action; approximate state may dominate exact belief planning.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** A useful transition/observation abstraction and enough information or learning to maintain it.

**Property ids.** EAA-005

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** State estimator and planner

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A useful transition/observation abstraction and enough information or learning to maintain it.

**Observable postcondition.** The estimator distinguishes action-relevant alternatives and exposes material model uncertainty.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Sufficiency of belief depends on a correct model; exact POMDP planning can be intractable and misplaced confidence can be worse than no model.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C002; EAA-C004

**Source ids.** EAA-S005; EAA-S021

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Sufficiency of belief depends on a correct model; exact POMDP planning can be intractable and misplaced confidence can be worse than no model.; Introduced cost or harm: Memory, computation, model-identification effort and approximation error.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Sufficiency of belief depends on a correct model; exact POMDP planning can be intractable and misplaced confidence can be worse than no model.

**Source ids.** EAA-S005; EAA-S021

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** What decision would fail without the extra state, and does the proposed representation actually resolve it?; Markov, observability and approximation assumptions must match the chosen model; a normalised belief is not external validation.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain representation adequacy as a conditional requirement, not completeness or a mandatory probability formalism.

**Condition.** Partial observability, uncertain transitions or delayed effects make an observation-only policy inadequate.

**Limit.** Sufficiency of belief depends on a correct model; exact POMDP planning can be intractable and misplaced confidence can be worse than no model.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

**Requalification analysis.** **Property id.** EAA-007

**Native name.** Adequate uncertainty representation rather than a complete world model

**Selected status.** ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE

**Constructed failure witness.** Two hidden modes give the same sensor reading but opposite safe actions, and a memoryless controller must choose the same action in both.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** Fully observed or suitably structured tasks can use a small reactive state; some compiled POMDP controllers avoid an explicit online belief vector.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain as assumption-sensitive. POMDP belief sufficiency is a model theorem, while Brooks establishes a serious alternative to full symbolic modelling. Neither establishes universal representation necessity or sufficiency.

## EAA-008 — Actionable information gathering

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-008

**Native name.** Actionable information gathering

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/7

**Original status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Selected status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Bearer and scope.** A sensing, query or clarification choice made before acting.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Evaluate the expected decision improvement against acquisition, delay and interaction costs over the remaining task horizon.

**Applicability guard.** An obtainable observation could change the chosen action, confidence threshold or justified abstention.

**Material premises.** The information can arrive before its value expires and its source is sufficiently informative.

**Evaluation rule.** Select information gathering when its decision value exceeds its total cost under the adopted criterion; do not reward information volume by itself.

**Constructed positive case.** A low-cost stock query prevents an otherwise likely futile purchase attempt.

**Constructed failure witness.** Repeatedly asking an already settled preference consumes the deadline without changing any admissible decision.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** Act directly when all possible query answers lead to the same action or when a conservative adequate action is cheaper.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain as context-dependent value-of-information reasoning. The cited models and interface principles explain the mechanism, but practical utilities and response distributions require local evidence.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S012; EAA-S014; EAA-S019; EAA-S045

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Information-gathering actions and dialogue selected for their decision value.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S012

**Date.** 1998

**Work.** Planning and Acting in Partially Observable Stochastic Domains

**Version.** Artificial Intelligence 101, 99–134

**Source id.** EAA-S014

**Date.** 1995-05

**Work.** Provably Bounded-Optimal Agents

**Version.** JAIR 2, 575–609

**Source id.** EAA-S019

**Date.** 1999

**Work.** Principles of Mixed-Initiative User Interfaces

**Version.** CHI 1999; author manuscript

**Source id.** EAA-S045

**Date.** 1996

**Work.** Planning-Based Control of Software Agents

**Version.** AIPS 1996 proceedings, pp.268–274

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Mechanism.** Treat sensing, tool queries or questions as candidate actions whose expected decision improvement is compared with delay, cost and disruption.

**Trigger or context.** An unresolved observation can change which feasible authorised action is preferable.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Skip the query when all plausible answers support the same action, or when a safe adequate action costs less.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** An available discriminating information source and a decision able to use the answer.

**Property ids.** EAA-007; EAA-010

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Planner or mixed-initiative decision maker

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. An available discriminating information source and a decision able to use the answer.

**Observable postcondition.** Obtained evidence changes or confirms an action-relevant distinction, with query costs included.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Value estimates can be wrong; repeated clarification can offload work onto humans or reveal private information.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C004

**Source ids.** EAA-S019; EAA-S021

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Value estimates can be wrong; repeated clarification can offload work onto humans or reveal private information.; Introduced cost or harm: Latency, attention, privacy and sensor/tool cost.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Value estimates can be wrong; repeated clarification can offload work onto humans or reveal private information.

**Source ids.** EAA-S019; EAA-S021

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** Which possible answer would change the decision, and is acquiring it worth its cost?; Information value is relative to the real decision and its deadline, not maximal knowledge.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Information gathering belongs inside bounded action selection rather than an obligatory preliminary phase.

**Condition.** An unresolved observation can change which feasible authorised action is preferable.

**Limit.** Value estimates can be wrong; repeated clarification can offload work onto humans or reveal private information.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

## EAA-009 — Abstention, waiting or safe non-action

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-009

**Native name.** Abstention, waiting or safe non-action

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/8

**Original status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Selected status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Bearer and scope.** A choice to wait, decline or take no immediate external action.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Judge consequences over the period of non-action and any deadline, not only the instant of abstention.

**Applicability guard.** Available actions are unjustified or an informative event may soon improve the choice.

**Material premises.** Waiting is itself feasible and its accumulating risk is assessed.

**Evaluation rule.** Permit non-action when it is the better supported option; evaluate delay losses and maintain a fallback if waiting can become unacceptable.

**Constructed positive case.** A robot pauses before an obstacle while obtaining a reliable range reading within a safe stopping envelope.

**Constructed failure witness.** A scheduling agent waits indefinitely for a silent user until the meeting can no longer be arranged; stopping a flying vehicle’s motors is not automatically safe.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** Use a preauthorized default or bounded reactive action when delay is more harmful than action.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain conditionally. Adjustable-autonomy cases show costly delay; physical-agent gating is only a small tested architecture. Abstention is not a universal safety guarantee.

## EAA-010 — Usable goal and success semantics

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-010

**Native name.** Usable goal and success semantics

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/9

**Original status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Selected status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Bearer and scope.** A task goal, its protected constraints and the evidence used to judge completion.

**Quantifier and horizon.** For each accepted task, distinguish terminal achievement, continuing conditions and process obligations over the declared horizon.

**Applicability guard.** The system is choosing or reporting actions in pursuit of a goal.

**Material premises.** An operationally meaningful criterion can be stated, or ambiguity is made explicit before consequential interpretation.

**Evaluation rule.** Relate the action plan and completion claim to the requested outcome and protected means; success on a surrogate alone is insufficient.

**Constructed positive case.** Booking a refundable flight requires both a reservation and the allowed fare conditions, with evidence of the resulting booking.

**Constructed failure witness.** A terminal database state is correct although the assistant skipped a required user confirmation.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A tiny deterministic transformation can use an ordinary function contract; no elaborate goal language is required.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. Planning and intention traditions supply explicit goals; WebArena and τ-bench expose concrete oracles and their limits; goal misgeneralization shows a trained proxy can diverge from intended behavior.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S006; EAA-S011; EAA-S025; EAA-S032; EAA-S033

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Symbolic goal conditions, adopted practical aims and later benchmark success predicates.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S006

**Date.** 1988

**Work.** Plans and Resource-Bounded Practical Reasoning

**Version.** Computational Intelligence 4, 349–355; 30-page author-formatted manuscript

**Source id.** EAA-S011

**Date.** 1971

**Work.** STRIPS: A New Approach to the Application of Theorem Proving to Problem Solving

**Version.** Artificial Intelligence 2, 189–208

**Source id.** EAA-S032

**Date.** 2024

**Version.** arXiv:2307.13854v4; initial submission 2023

**Source id.** EAA-S033

**Date.** 2024-06

**Work.** τ-bench: A Benchmark for Tool-Agent-User Interaction in Real-World Domains

**Version.** arXiv:2406.12045v1

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Mechanism.** Specify the relevant desired condition, scope, constraints and completion evidence; distinguish preferences from obligations and terminal achievement from continued maintenance.

**Trigger or context.** Goal adoption, interpreting a task, judging completion or revising a plan.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A simple visible condition can be sufficient; do not require a formal utility function for an unambiguous bounded task.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** An interpretable task and a consumer of the completion decision.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Delegator, planner and evaluator

**Boundary.** This is a research/evaluation criterion or rejected inference; it does not itself confer operative authority. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. An interpretable task and a consumer of the completion decision.

**Observable postcondition.** Observed success can be assessed against the intended condition rather than fluent narration or an unexamined score.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Even terminal database equality can miss prohibited means or absent confirmation; a measurable proxy is not the whole objective.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C006

**Source ids.** EAA-S025; EAA-S033

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Even terminal database equality can miss prohibited means or absent confirmation; a measurable proxy is not the whole objective.; Introduced cost or harm: Specification effort and possible overconstraint.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Even terminal database equality can miss prohibited means or absent confirmation; a measurable proxy is not the whole objective.

**Source ids.** EAA-S025; EAA-S033

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** What establishes fulfilment, and what successful-looking result would still fail the task?; Not all values can be fully specified; uncertainty must not silently grant authority to rewrite them.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Couple desired outcomes with relevant process constraints and a usable success oracle, leaving genuinely unresolved task meaning explicit.

**Condition.** Goal adoption, interpreting a task, judging completion or revising a plan.

**Limit.** Even terminal database equality can miss prohibited means or absent confirmation; a measurable proxy is not the whole objective.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

<a id="eaa-011"></a>

## EAA-011 — Achievement and maintenance commitments are not interchangeable

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-011

**Native name.** Achievement and maintenance commitments are not interchangeable

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/10

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** An agent commitment to reach a condition or keep it within a required region.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Achievement is evaluated at an eligible attainment time; maintenance throughout its specified interval or at explicitly sampled boundaries.

**Applicability guard.** The difference affects which actions can count as success.

**Material premises.** The time interval, observability and tolerated excursions are defined; continuous guarantees cannot be inferred from sparse samples.

**Evaluation rule.** Do not retire a maintenance obligation merely because it was once satisfied, and do not require an achieved one-shot goal to remain active without reason.

**Constructed positive case.** Reach a charging station once, while maintaining enough fuel to remain recoverable until arrival.

**Constructed failure witness.** A controller declares fuel maintenance complete after one sufficient reading, then executes a route that exhausts the reserve.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** For a single terminal transformation, an achievement condition suffices; predictive maintenance is optional when reactive protection is adequate.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. Maintenance-goal models and simulations distinguish proactive from reactive control and report prediction-error costs. They do not prove prediction is always superior or that the study’s finite simulation establishes continuous invariance.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S051

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Achievement goals contrasted with continuing maintenance; reactive restoration and predictive prevention.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S051

**Date.** 2012-07-24

**Work.** Maintenance Goals in Intelligent Agents

**Version.** Accepted author manuscript; final Computational Intelligence 30(1), 71–114 issue 2014; DOI 10.1111/coin.12000

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Mechanism.** Distinguish reaching a goal from maintaining a condition; retain monitoring after restoration where required, using predictive intervention only when the prediction is sufficiently credible.

**Trigger or context.** Resource bounds, persistent service conditions or other continuing objectives.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A one-shot achievement criterion suffices for genuinely terminal tasks; reactive restoration may be cheaper than prediction.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** A time horizon or continuing scope, observable maintenance condition and feasible response to a threatened violation.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Goal manager and execution monitor

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A time horizon or continuing scope, observable maintenance condition and feasible response to a threatened violation.

**Observable postcondition.** The agent does not retire a maintenance obligation merely because the condition temporarily holds.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Predictive maintenance with inaccurate resource models may reject feasible goals or strand the agent; continuous vigilance also has cost.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C003

**Source ids.** EAA-S051

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Predictive maintenance with inaccurate resource models may reject feasible goals or strand the agent; continuous vigilance also has cost.; Introduced cost or harm: Monitoring overhead and unnecessary preventive action.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Predictive maintenance with inaccurate resource models may reject feasible goals or strand the agent; continuous vigilance also has cost.

**Source ids.** EAA-S051

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** Is success terminal, or must the condition continue to hold after restoration?; Predictions and resource summaries must be valid enough for the task; source experiments are simulated.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Choose reactive or proactive maintenance using model quality, lead time and violation cost, not a universal preference for anticipation.

**Condition.** Resource bounds, persistent service conditions or other continuing objectives.

**Limit.** Predictive maintenance with inaccurate resource models may reject feasible goals or strand the agent; continuous vigilance also has cost.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

## EAA-012 — Concurrent-goal interference protection

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-012

**Native name.** Concurrent-goal interference protection

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/11

**Original status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Selected status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Bearer and scope.** Concurrent goals, plans or intentions sharing conditions or resources in one agent.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Across overlapping execution windows, considering the interactions that can invalidate protected conditions.

**Applicability guard.** Independent pursuit can consume a shared resource or establish/delete another plan’s precondition.

**Material premises.** Relevant effects, resource use or conflict indicators are knowable enough to schedule or arbitrate.

**Evaluation rule.** Detect or conservatively prevent material interference, and assess deadlock or starvation introduced by the remedy.

**Constructed positive case.** Two goals compete for a vehicle; the scheduler serializes only the overlapping use and permits independent preparation.

**Constructed failure witness.** One plan deletes a condition another still relies on; a universal lock then prevents either plan from completing.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A single goal or serial execution with no shared mutable resource needs no general interference manager.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain conditionally. Thangarajah and colleagues give mechanisms for single-agent concurrent achievement goals; their assumed plan annotations and scope do not solve arbitrary multi-agent coordination or every priority policy.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S006; EAA-S054

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Concurrent goal plans summarised by possible/definite conditions and effects in a known hierarchy.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S006

**Date.** 1988

**Work.** Plans and Resource-Bounded Practical Reasoning

**Version.** Computational Intelligence 4, 349–355; 30-page author-formatted manuscript

**Source id.** EAA-S054

**Date.** 2003

**Work.** Detecting & Avoiding Interference Between Goals in Intelligent Agents

**Version.** IJCAI 2003, pp.721–726

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Mechanism.** Use task-relevant condition/effect/resource summaries or another adequate conflict test to prevent mutually destructive concurrent commitments and to revise summaries as execution proceeds.

**Trigger or context.** Parallel goals or plans share mutable conditions, resources or deadlines.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** No conflict machinery is necessary for a single isolated goal or demonstrably independent actions; serialisation can be adequate if affordable.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** Known or conservatively bounded effects, applicable priorities and an arbiter that can defer or decline conflicting work.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Goal manager and scheduler

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Known or conservatively bounded effects, applicable priorities and an arbiter that can defer or decline conflicting work.

**Observable postcondition.** Concurrent execution preserves the protected conditions or makes the unresolved conflict explicit before preventable interference.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Overapproximated summaries block benign concurrency; omitted effects miss real conflicts; an achievement-goal method is not automatically a maintenance-goal proof.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C005

**Source ids.** EAA-S054

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Overapproximated summaries block benign concurrency; omitted effects miss real conflicts; an achievement-goal method is not automatically a maintenance-goal proof.; Introduced cost or harm: Computation, reduced concurrency and priority disputes.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Overapproximated summaries block benign concurrency; omitted effects miss real conflicts; an achievement-goal method is not automatically a maintenance-goal proof.

**Source ids.** EAA-S054

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** Which simultaneous intentions can invalidate one another, and what evidence justifies treating them as independent?; Known plan hierarchies and reliable effect summaries delimit the original method; unknown tools require additional evidence.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Preserve interference discrimination while selecting the cheapest adequate analysis, isolation or scheduling mechanism.

**Condition.** Parallel goals or plans share mutable conditions, resources or deadlines.

**Limit.** Overapproximated summaries block benign concurrency; omitted effects miss real conflicts; an achievement-goal method is not automatically a maintenance-goal proof.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True
