## EAA-013 — Conditional intention commitment

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-013

**Native name.** Conditional intention commitment

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/12

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** An intention or persistent plan commitment in a resource-bounded agent.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Persist between material reconsideration events, subject to success, impossibility belief, changed relevance or explicit budget/authority limits.

**Applicability guard.** Replanning every cycle would waste work or undermine coordination, while some changes can invalidate the commitment.

**Material premises.** Commitment and its release conditions have operational meaning; belief in impossibility remains fallible.

**Evaluation rule.** Maintain enough stability to support action but release a commitment when its stated conditions cease to hold.

**Constructed positive case.** Keep the selected route through minor sensor fluctuations, but reconsider when the bridge closes.

**Constructed failure witness.** A rigid agent pursues a cancelled task forever; a fickle one restarts planning after every harmless observation.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A one-step reflex action does not need a persistent intention structure.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain in evolved conditional form. Bratman’s resource-bounded account, BDI models and Cohen–Levesque persistence motivate the tradeoff. Tileworld supplies a bounded experimental discriminator, not one best commitment policy for every environment.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S006; EAA-S008; EAA-S009; EAA-S015; EAA-S052

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Adopted plans constrain later reasoning; alternative formal accounts specify different persistence/termination conditions.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S006

**Date.** 1988

**Work.** Plans and Resource-Bounded Practical Reasoning

**Version.** Computational Intelligence 4, 349–355; 30-page author-formatted manuscript

**Source id.** EAA-S008

**Date.** 1991-02

**Work.** Modeling Rational Agents within a BDI-Architecture

**Version.** Australian AI Institute Technical Note 14; KR 1991 antecedent

**Source id.** EAA-S009

**Date.** 1995

**Work.** BDI Agents: From Theory to Practice

**Version.** ICMAS 1995 pp.312–319

**Source id.** EAA-S052

**Date.** 1990-05-01

**Work.** On Acting Together

**Version.** SRI Technical Note 485, submitted to AAAI 1990; scanned author report

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Mechanism.** Retain an adopted intention as a constraint on deliberation until achievement, recognised impossibility, loss of relevance or authorised priority change warrants release.

**Trigger or context.** Multi-step activity under changing opportunities and finite deliberation resources.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** No persistent intention store is needed for a single reactive action; reconsider immediately when continuation is plainly invalid.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** Goal identity, progress evidence and a means of recognising termination grounds.

**Property ids.** EAA-014; EAA-015

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Practical-reasoning and execution process

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Goal identity, progress evidence and a means of recognising termination grounds.

**Observable postcondition.** Behaviour persists through irrelevant distractions but releases an intention when its governing grounds cease to hold.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Formal perfect introspection and consistency are not implementation facts; fixed blind/single/open-minded strategies are not universally optimal.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C003

**Source ids.** EAA-S009; EAA-S015

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Formal perfect introspection and consistency are not implementation facts; fixed blind/single/open-minded strategies are not universally optimal.; Introduced cost or harm: Missed opportunities or commitment churn if termination tests are poor.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Formal perfect introspection and consistency are not implementation facts; fixed blind/single/open-minded strategies are not universally optimal.

**Source ids.** EAA-S009; EAA-S015

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** What stabilises the intention, and what specific event is sufficient to abandon it?; Different formal schools represent intention differently; this synthesis does not prove their logics equivalent.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Preserve commitment’s coordination role but make its retention and termination conditions operational and resource-aware.

**Condition.** Multi-step activity under changing opportunities and finite deliberation resources.

**Limit.** Formal perfect introspection and consistency are not implementation facts; fixed blind/single/open-minded strategies are not universally optimal.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

**Requalification analysis.** **Property id.** EAA-013

**Native name.** Conditional intention commitment

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/12

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** An intention or persistent plan commitment in a resource-bounded agent.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Persist between material reconsideration events, subject to success, impossibility belief, changed relevance or explicit budget/authority limits.

**Applicability guard.** Replanning every cycle would waste work or undermine coordination, while some changes can invalidate the commitment.

**Material premises.** Commitment and its release conditions have operational meaning; belief in impossibility remains fallible.

**Evaluation rule.** Maintain enough stability to support action but release a commitment when its stated conditions cease to hold.

**Constructed positive case.** Keep the selected route through minor sensor fluctuations, but reconsider when the bridge closes.

**Constructed failure witness.** A rigid agent pursues a cancelled task forever; a fickle one restarts planning after every harmless observation.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A one-step reflex action does not need a persistent intention structure.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain in evolved conditional form. Bratman’s resource-bounded account, BDI models and Cohen–Levesque persistence motivate the tradeoff. Tileworld supplies a bounded experimental discriminator, not one best commitment policy for every environment.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S006; EAA-S008; EAA-S009; EAA-S015; EAA-S052

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.


<a id="eaa-014"></a>

## EAA-014 — Economical reconsideration scheduling

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-014

**Native name.** Economical reconsideration scheduling

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/13

**Original status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Selected status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Bearer and scope.** The schedule for reconsidering commitments or plans.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Over a task with finite reasoning capacity and environmental change; evaluate both response delay and reasoning overhead.

**Applicability guard.** Reconsideration can alter a decision and has non-negligible cost.

**Material premises.** There is a usable event trigger, periodic schedule or expected-value approximation.

**Evaluation rule.** Choose a schedule that balances missed changes against wasted deliberation under the actual task’s volatility and cost envelope.

**Constructed positive case.** A material goal cancellation triggers immediate reconsideration while static route segments are not replanned.

**Constructed failure witness.** Continuous replanning consumes all available time, or an infrequent fixed schedule misses a rapidly closing opportunity.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** Use a cheap periodic check when it is adequate; a separate optimizing metareasoner is unnecessary.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain context dependence. Tileworld’s changing-world experiments and bounded-optimality arguments establish why cost and volatility matter, not a universal optimal frequency.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S006; EAA-S014; EAA-S015

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Fixed commitment/reconsideration strategies compared under varied thinking cost and environmental change.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S006

**Date.** 1988

**Work.** Plans and Resource-Bounded Practical Reasoning

**Version.** Computational Intelligence 4, 349–355; 30-page author-formatted manuscript

**Source id.** EAA-S014

**Date.** 1995-05

**Work.** Provably Bounded-Optimal Agents

**Version.** JAIR 2, 575–609

**Source id.** EAA-S015

**Date.** 1991

**Work.** Commitment and Effectiveness of Situated Agents

**Version.** IJCAI 1991 pp.82–88

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Mechanism.** Choose reconsideration opportunities using environmental change, action horizon, planning cost and failure consequence; use event triggers or cheap screens where sufficient.

**Trigger or context.** Changing options or state during a persistent plan.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Keep the current plan when new information cannot change its adequacy; use a fixed schedule only where justified by the setting.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** A working continuation policy and some discrimination between relevant and irrelevant changes.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Deliberation scheduler

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A working continuation policy and some discrimination between relevant and irrelevant changes.

**Observable postcondition.** Reconsideration can alter a decision when worthwhile without automatically consuming every available action window.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Tileworld reversals depend on deliberation cost and change rate; there is no empirically universal reconsideration frequency.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C003

**Source ids.** EAA-S015

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Tileworld reversals depend on deliberation cost and change rate; there is no empirically universal reconsideration frequency.; Introduced cost or harm: Opportunity cost of reasoning and risk of delayed response.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Tileworld reversals depend on deliberation cost and change rate; there is no empirically universal reconsideration frequency.

**Source ids.** EAA-S015

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** When would another planning step be less useful than acting on the current adequate plan?; Value-of-reconsideration estimates can themselves be expensive and uncertain.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain a guarded scheduling problem, not permanent reflection or a fixed universal depth.

**Condition.** Changing options or state during a persistent plan.

**Limit.** Tileworld reversals depend on deliberation cost and change rate; there is no empirically universal reconsideration frequency.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

<a id="eaa-015"></a>

## EAA-015 — Retirement of obsolete, unreachable or exhausted goals

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-015

**Native name.** Retirement of obsolete, unreachable or exhausted goals

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/14

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** An active goal or intention whose justification, reachability or allocated budget may lapse.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Evaluate at specified retirement events and through remaining cleanup obligations; finite non-completion alone does not prove impossibility.

**Applicability guard.** A task is achieved, irrelevant, believed unattainable with adequate evidence, cancelled or beyond its authorized resource envelope.

**Material premises.** The retirement reason is distinguished from a single failed plan and residual obligations are identified.

**Evaluation rule.** Stop spending on a retired goal and discharge or transfer material remaining commitments; do not turn a plan failure into an unsupported impossibility claim.

**Constructed positive case.** After a user cancels a trip, stop booking attempts and handle an already-created refundable reservation within the permitted scope.

**Constructed failure witness.** The first route fails, so the agent declares the destination unreachable although another permitted route exists.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A finite one-shot function terminates normally without a general goal-retirement subsystem.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. Persistent-goal logic, maintenance-goal resource bounds and adjustable-autonomy delays supply different retirement mechanisms. Their semantics and horizons must not be collapsed.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S020; EAA-S051; EAA-S052

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Persistent-goal termination on achievement, impossibility or loss of relevance; later resource-sensitive failure examples.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S052

**Date.** 1990-05-01

**Work.** On Acting Together

**Version.** SRI Technical Note 485, submitted to AAAI 1990; scanned author report

**Source id.** EAA-S051

**Date.** 2012-07-24

**Work.** Maintenance Goals in Intelligent Agents

**Version.** Accepted author manuscript; final Computational Intelligence 30(1), 71–114 issue 2014; DOI 10.1111/coin.12000

**Source id.** EAA-S020

**Date.** 2002

**Work.** Towards Adjustable Autonomy for the Real World

**Version.** JAIR 17, 171–228; author manuscript paginated 1–58

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Mechanism.** Use relevance, feasibility, progress and budget evidence to suspend or retire pursuit; distinguish failure of one plan from impossibility of the goal and report material non-fulfilment.

**Trigger or context.** Changed task, repeated unproductive attempts, exhausted resources or established infeasibility.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Continue without a new retirement procedure when existing bounded execution already enforces the stop condition.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** A legitimate goal source, evidence of progress/failure and an executable stop or handback path.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Goal manager and delegator

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A legitimate goal source, evidence of progress/failure and an executable stop or handback path.

**Observable postcondition.** An obsolete or unsupported pursuit ceases rather than silently exhausting the environment or resources.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Premature abandonment loses achievable goals; a wrong model can label a feasible task impossible.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C003

**Source ids.** EAA-S051; EAA-S020

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Premature abandonment loses achievable goals; a wrong model can label a feasible task impossible.; Introduced cost or harm: Lost opportunities and human rework.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Premature abandonment loses achievable goals; a wrong model can label a feasible task impossible.

**Source ids.** EAA-S051; EAA-S020

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** Which evidence distinguishes a failed attempt from a goal that should no longer be pursued?; Impossibility beliefs remain fallible; “not achievable by this plan/budget” need not mean impossible in general.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Termination is evidence- and authority-sensitive, with explicit uncertainty rather than a success claim after giving up.

**Condition.** Changed task, repeated unproductive attempts, exhausted resources or established infeasibility.

**Limit.** Premature abandonment loses achievable goals; a wrong model can label a feasible task impossible.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

**Requalification analysis.** **Property id.** EAA-015

**Native name.** Retirement of obsolete, unreachable or exhausted goals

**Constructed failure witness.** The first route fails, so the agent declares the destination unreachable although another permitted route exists.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. Persistent-goal logic, maintenance-goal resource bounds and adjustable-autonomy delays supply different retirement mechanisms. Their semantics and horizons must not be collapsed.

## EAA-016 — Direct reactive control as a legitimate architecture branch

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-016

**Native name.** Direct reactive control as a legitimate architecture branch

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/15

**Original status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Selected status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Bearer and scope.** A direct sensor-to-action or small-state reactive controller.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Adequacy is relative to the operational envelope and required response times, not to every possible task.

**Applicability guard.** Local feedback is sufficient for the needed decisions and rapid response has value.

**Material premises.** Sensing, actuation and interaction between behaviors are adequate for the operating conditions.

**Evaluation rule.** Evaluate task performance and failure boundaries directly; do not reject the controller merely because it lacks central symbolic deliberation.

**Constructed positive case.** Obstacle avoidance reacts to range readings while the environment remains within its tested geometry and speed envelope.

**Constructed failure witness.** A locally reactive rule cycles between obstacles because the task requires remembering a previous failed route.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** Keep the small controller when it meets the task; add memory or planning only for a demonstrated missing distinction.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain as a genuine architecture branch. Brooks supplies an explicit research alternative and layered mechanism; later hybrid systems are compatible with that critique without establishing that reaction is universally sufficient.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S004; EAA-S005; EAA-S016; EAA-S046

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Task-achieving asynchronous behaviour layers and reactive action methods.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S004

**Date.** 1985-09

**Work.** A Robust Layered Control System for a Mobile Robot

**Version.** MIT AI Memo 864; antecedent of 1986 journal article

**Source id.** EAA-S005

**Date.** 1991

**Work.** Intelligence without representation

**Version.** Artificial Intelligence 47, 139–159; author reformatted copy

**Source id.** EAA-S016

**Date.** 1987

**Work.** An Investigation into Reactive Planning in Complex Domains

**Version.** AAAI 1987 pp.202–206

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Mechanism.** Couple task-relevant observations to behaviours with explicit arbitration or inhibition where needed; let the world provide feedback instead of reconstructing it gratuitously.

**Trigger or context.** Tight response deadlines, adequate local observability and behaviour-level objectives.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A fixed control rule can be the complete solution; add state or planning only for demonstrated aliasing, conflict or horizon needs.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** Observable cues reliably connected to adequate actions and manageable interaction among behaviours.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Controller designer and runtime action selector

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Observable cues reliably connected to adequate actions and manageable interaction among behaviours.

**Observable postcondition.** The agent responds effectively within the action window without depending on avoidable deliberation.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Pure reaction can fail at delayed consequences, hidden state or non-local goals; suppression among behaviours can conceal priorities.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C002

**Source ids.** EAA-S012; EAA-S046

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Pure reaction can fail at delayed consequences, hidden state or non-local goals; suppression among behaviours can conceal priorities.; Introduced cost or harm: Limited foresight and behavioural interaction debugging.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Pure reaction can fail at delayed consequences, hidden state or non-local goals; suppression among behaviours can conceal priorities.

**Source ids.** EAA-S012; EAA-S046

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** Can a direct policy satisfy the task as reliably and cheaply as a planner?; Behaviour adequacy is scoped to environment and goals, not a universal representation-free guarantee.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain reactive control as an alternative and possible subsystem, not a primitive stage everyone must outgrow.

**Condition.** Tight response deadlines, adequate local observability and behaviour-level objectives.

**Limit.** Pure reaction can fail at delayed consequences, hidden state or non-local goals; suppression among behaviours can conceal priorities.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

## EAA-017 — Model-based deliberation when anticipation earns its cost

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-017

**Native name.** Model-based deliberation when anticipation earns its cost

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/16

**Original status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Selected status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Bearer and scope.** A deliberative model and planner used to anticipate action consequences.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Over a specified planning and execution horizon under bounded computation.

**Applicability guard.** Anticipation can improve an action beyond a credible reactive or fixed policy and its cost is affordable.

**Material premises.** Model errors and execution interfaces are managed; a plan is not confused with a completed action.

**Evaluation rule.** Compare the value of anticipatory choices with modelling, search, sensing and staleness costs in the intended task.

**Constructed positive case.** A robot chooses a longer route that preserves fuel and avoids an irreversible dead end.

**Constructed failure witness.** A detailed plan optimizes an obsolete map while a simple controller could safely finish the local task.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A fixed workflow or reactive rule is adequate when the environment is predictable or choices are trivial.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain conditionally. STRIPS explicitly distinguishes model operators from action routines, so the planning tradition must not be caricatured as ignoring execution. POMDP and software-agent planning support anticipation under different assumptions; Brooks and DS1 expose practical limits.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S011; EAA-S012; EAA-S045; EAA-S005; EAA-S022

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Search over represented operator conditions and effects, later including uncertain belief-state decisions.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S011

**Date.** 1971

**Work.** STRIPS: A New Approach to the Application of Theorem Proving to Problem Solving

**Version.** Artificial Intelligence 2, 189–208

**Source id.** EAA-S012

**Date.** 1998

**Work.** Planning and Acting in Partially Observable Stochastic Domains

**Version.** Artificial Intelligence 101, 99–134

**Source id.** EAA-S045

**Date.** 1996

**Work.** Planning-Based Control of Software Agents

**Version.** AIPS 1996 proceedings, pp.268–274

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Mechanism.** Search or evaluate action sequences using explicit conditions, effects and objectives; expose uncertainty and pass plans to monitored execution rather than treating proof of a plan as fulfilment.

**Trigger or context.** Non-local goals, dependencies or contingencies that a cheap direct policy cannot adequately handle.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use a validated fixed plan, policy or reactive response when search adds no useful discrimination.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** Adequate action/state model, a defined objective and computation available before the action opportunity closes.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Planner and executor

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Adequate action/state model, a defined objective and computation available before the action opportunity closes.

**Observable postcondition.** A selected plan is justified under stated assumptions and executable capabilities, with remaining model uncertainty visible.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Model omission, computational expense and state change undermine practical benefit; original STRIPS already acknowledges execution issues, so “planning never monitors” is a caricature.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C002

**Source ids.** EAA-S005; EAA-S014; EAA-S022

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Model omission, computational expense and state change undermine practical benefit; original STRIPS already acknowledges execution issues, so “planning never monitors” is a caricature.; Introduced cost or harm: Search cost, stale state and model-maintenance effort.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Model omission, computational expense and state change undermine practical benefit; original STRIPS already acknowledges execution issues, so “planning never monitors” is a caricature.

**Source ids.** EAA-S005; EAA-S014; EAA-S022

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** Which failure of the cheaper policy requires look-ahead, and is the model adequate for it?; Guarantees are relative to model adequacy and search approximation.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain bounded model-based anticipation coupled to execution, not planning as a sufficient account of agency.

**Condition.** Non-local goals, dependencies or contingencies that a cheap direct policy cannot adequately handle.

**Limit.** Model omission, computational expense and state change undermine practical benefit; original STRIPS already acknowledges execution issues, so “planning never monitors” is a caricature.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

## EAA-018 — Executable BDI and plan-library interpretation

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-018

**Original status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Selected status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Bearer and scope.** An operational belief–desire–intention architecture with an actual interpreter and plan library.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Across the interpreter’s stated event, plan-selection and intention-execution transitions.

**Applicability guard.** Persistent commitments and reusable context-sensitive plans are appropriate to the task.

**Material premises.** The implemented semantics, selection functions and resource limits are specified; abstract logical closure assumptions are not silently attributed to the implementation.

**Evaluation rule.** Trace beliefs and events to applicable plans and execution, including failure and reconsideration behavior.

**Constructed positive case.** An event selects an applicable plan, creates an intention, and releases it under documented failure handling.

**Constructed failure witness.** A system uses BDI names but has no defined event processing or commitment lifecycle.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** Use a simpler state machine or reactive controller if it expresses the needed behavior with less burden.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain as an architectural option. Rao–Georgeff and AgentSpeak provide operational accounts, while their formal models do not automatically transfer to every practical interpreter.

**Bearer and scope.** An operational belief–desire–intention architecture with an actual interpreter and plan library.

**Evaluation rule.** Trace beliefs and events to applicable plans and execution, including failure and reconsideration behavior.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** Use a simpler state machine or reactive controller if it expresses the needed behavior with less burden.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain as an architectural option. Rao–Georgeff and AgentSpeak provide operational accounts, while their formal models do not automatically transfer to every practical interpreter.

## EAA-019 — Hybrid arbitration and interface coherence

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-019

**Native name.** Hybrid arbitration and interface coherence

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/18

**Original status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Selected status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Bearer and scope.** A hybrid controller whose reactive, sequencing and deliberative components share effects.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Across command handoffs and overlapping control timescales.

**Applicability guard.** More than one layer or policy can issue conflicting commands or rely on shared state.

**Material premises.** Arbitration, feedback and cancellation semantics can be identified.

**Evaluation rule.** Prevent incompatible commands and maintain coherent handoff information; measure latency and failure introduced by arbitration itself.

**Constructed positive case.** A safety controller suppresses a planner’s motion command while the sequencer records the interruption and replans appropriately.

**Constructed failure witness.** The reactive layer stops a motion but the planner assumes arrival and dispatches a dependent action.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** One adequate controller avoids interlayer coordination; a two-layer design need not imitate a canonical three-layer architecture.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain conditionally. Brooks’ suppression mechanism and 3T’s experiences support different hybrids. DS1 warns that composed software can fail at integration boundaries despite individually reasonable components.

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

<a id="eaa-020"></a>

## EAA-020 — Smallest adequate architecture and credible simpler baseline

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-020

**Native name.** Smallest adequate architecture and credible simpler baseline

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/19

**Original status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Selected status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Bearer and scope.** The architecture selected for a stated task, resource envelope and protected outcomes.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Compare at the required performance and risk scope, not by minimum component count alone.

**Applicability guard.** There are materially different candidate architectures or an incumbent already doing the job.

**Material premises.** The simpler comparator has a fair task, tool, model, cost and information budget or the mismatch is disclosed.

**Evaluation rule.** Retain additional machinery only when it addresses a demonstrated requirement or yields justified benefit relative to its cost and failure exposure.

**Constructed positive case.** A fixed software-repair pipeline with bounded retries meets the benchmark requirement without a persistent agent loop.

**Constructed failure witness.** A costly multi-stage agent is declared superior to a single attempt while being allowed many more samples.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** No change is legitimate when the incumbent meets the requirement and new complexity has no demonstrated value.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain strongly as a comparison discipline, not a theorem that smallest is always best. AI Agents That Matter supplies controlled sample-budget criticism; Agentless supplies a concrete simpler alternative with its own nontrivial sampling cost and reported-baseline limitations.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S005; EAA-S046; EAA-S035; EAA-S036

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

## EAA-021 — Ground actions in executable capabilities and affordances

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-021

**Native name.** Ground actions in executable capabilities and affordances

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/20

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** A proposed physical or software action connected to an executable skill or tool.

**Quantifier and horizon.** At selection and dispatch, for the particular tool, state and arguments.

**Applicability guard.** A language-level or symbolic action can be proposed without a corresponding feasible execution.

**Material premises.** The skill exists, arguments have usable meaning, and relevant environmental preconditions can be estimated; authorization is separately established.

**Evaluation rule.** Bind the proposal to a capability whose preconditions and effects are meaningful in the current context. Treat estimated affordance as uncertain feasibility evidence.

**Constructed positive case.** A planner selects an available grasp skill for an object within reach and a permitted destination.

**Constructed failure witness.** A plausible instruction calls an unavailable tool or treats a high skill score as permission to enter a restricted room.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A fixed workflow with statically bound functions may already provide adequate grounding.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. STRIPS action routines, planning-based software effectors and SayCan make the model-to-action link concrete. SayCan’s skill-conditioned scores and physical-agent tests establish bounded mechanisms, not guaranteed feasibility or authority.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S011; EAA-S045; EAA-S049; EAA-S039

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Version.** arXiv:2204.01691v2; initial submission April 2022

**Work.** Physical Agentic AI: An Architecture for Orchestrating a Robot Crew with LLMs

**Current status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Mechanism.** Map proposed actions to actual capability interfaces and task-relevant feasibility estimates; reject invented actions and distinguish a skill’s availability from its likely success.

**Trigger or context.** Planning with tools, robots or dynamically available capabilities.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A fixed typed action set is enough where availability and effects are already established; learned affordance scoring is not mandatory.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** A known executor interface, applicable skill definitions and evidence about relevant conditions and effects.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. **Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** SayCan’s skill grounding improves discrimination in tested kitchens but does not close the planning/execution gap; capability does not confer permission.

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Limit.** SayCan’s skill grounding improves discrimination in tested kitchens but does not close the planning/execution gap; capability does not confer permission.

## EAA-022 — Recheck material action conditions at dispatch

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-022

**Native name.** Recheck material action conditions at dispatch

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/21

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** An action dispatch together with the material conditions that justify it.

**Quantifier and horizon.** At the last meaningful commitment point and over any vulnerable interval before the effect; validation need not be an extra call if atomicity already supplies it.

**Applicability guard.** Conditions may change between planning and execution in a way that changes feasibility, permission or protected outcomes.

**Material premises.** The check is relevant and sufficiently current; remaining check-to-use races and observation uncertainty are bounded or disclosed.

**Evaluation rule.** Re-establish the material conditions or use an atomic/conditional operation that enforces them; do not mistake a stale passed check for current validity.

**Constructed positive case.** A reservation update uses a version-conditional write so a concurrent change aborts the action.

**Constructed failure witness.** A resource is available at planning time but consumed before dispatch, while the action proceeds from the old assumption.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A stable pure function or atomic operation can make an additional recheck redundant.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. Reactive planning and physical dispatch gating make this mechanism explicit. The newer robot study tests a limited set of faults; neither it nor DS1 establishes that all races or uncertainties are eliminated.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S011; EAA-S016; EAA-S022; EAA-S039

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Version.** Artificial Intelligence 2, 189–208

**Version.** AAAI 1987 pp.202–206

**Date.** 2026-08-23

**Work.** Physical Agentic AI: An Architecture for Orchestrating a Robot Crew with LLMs

**Version.** arXiv:2608.22657v1

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Mechanism.** At the consequential dispatch boundary, check the still-relevant feasibility, state and delegated constraints; reject, refresh or refer invalid requests rather than relying only on earlier planning.

**Trigger or context.** Mutable conditions, learned/generated plans, significant delays or consequential actions.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use existing type/actuator checks or an atomic constrained operation when these already establish the required conditions.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** Inspectable material conditions, a real interception point and an available outcome for failed checks.

**Property ids.** EAA-004; EAA-006; EAA-021

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Executor or capability boundary

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Inspectable material conditions, a real interception point and an available outcome for failed checks.

**Observable postcondition.** No action is knowingly dispatched on a material failed check; residual unobservable conditions are not represented as verified.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Checks based on stale or omitted state can pass incorrectly; the 2026 simulation’s zero observed false dispatch does not establish universal safety.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C005; EAA-C006

**Source ids.** EAA-S022; EAA-S039

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Checks based on stale or omitted state can pass incorrectly; the 2026 simulation’s zero observed false dispatch does not establish universal safety.; Introduced cost or harm: Delay, false blocking and dependency on the checking mechanism.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Checks based on stale or omitted state can pass incorrectly; the 2026 simulation’s zero observed false dispatch does not establish universal safety.

**Source ids.** EAA-S022; EAA-S039

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** Which conditions must still hold when the action begins, and where are they actually checked?; A check-to-use race remains unless timing/atomicity is adequate; unmodelled preconditions are not automatically detected.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain action-time validation with honest sensing limits, not a universal gate that supposedly eliminates every unknown risk.

**Condition.** Mutable conditions, learned/generated plans, significant delays or consequential actions.

**Limit.** Checks based on stale or omitted state can pass incorrectly; the 2026 simulation’s zero observed false dispatch does not establish universal safety.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

**Applicability guard.** Conditions may change between planning and execution in a way that changes feasibility, permission or protected outcomes.

**Material premises.** The check is relevant and sufficiently current; remaining check-to-use races and observation uncertainty are bounded or disclosed.

**Evaluation rule.** Re-establish the material conditions or use an atomic/conditional operation that enforces them; do not mistake a stale passed check for current validity.

**Constructed positive case.** A reservation update uses a version-conditional write so a concurrent change aborts the action.

**Constructed failure witness.** A resource is available at planning time but consumed before dispatch, while the action proceeds from the old assumption.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A stable pure function or atomic operation can make an additional recheck redundant.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. Reactive planning and physical dispatch gating make this mechanism explicit. The newer robot study tests a limited set of faults; neither it nor DS1 establishes that all races or uncertainties are eliminated.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S011; EAA-S016; EAA-S022; EAA-S039

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

## EAA-023 — Distinguish issuance, acknowledgement and established effect

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-023

**Native name.** Distinguish issuance, acknowledgement and established effect

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/22

**Original status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Selected status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Bearer and scope.** An action request, acknowledgement and asserted environmental outcome.

**Quantifier and horizon.** For each material completion claim, at the time it is made and relative to the required outcome horizon.

**Applicability guard.** A request can be accepted without completing, or its effect can fail, be partial or be undone.

**Material premises.** There is an adequate outcome signal, observation or justified atomic contract; its scope is understood.

**Evaluation rule.** Claim only the strongest state established by evidence: issued, accepted, pending, failed or effect confirmed. A return value can suffice when its documented semantics establish the required effect.

**Constructed positive case.** A booking API returns a committed reservation identifier whose contract establishes the booking; no redundant manual verification is needed.

**Constructed failure witness.** A queued email API returns accepted and the assistant reports delivery although the queue later rejects the message.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A synchronous atomic local operation may combine acknowledgement and established effect.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain strongly. Execution monitoring, DS1, WebArena and τ-bench support the distinction. This is task-instance evidence; population evaluation and policy-oracle adequacy remain a separate question in EAA-045.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S016; EAA-S022; EAA-S032; EAA-S033

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Execution monitors and result/state checks distinguish intended effects from command issue.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S016

**Date.** 1987

**Work.** An Investigation into Reactive Planning in Complex Domains

**Version.** AAAI 1987 pp.202–206

**Source id.** EAA-S022

**Date.** 1999

**Date.** 2024-06

**Version.** arXiv:2406.12045v1

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Mechanism.** Interpret acknowledgement according to its actual semantics; observe the task-relevant effect or retain uncertainty when it cannot be established, and use that result in subsequent action and reporting.

**Trigger or context.** Actions with asynchronous, fallible, delayed or externally mediated effects.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A trustworthy atomic operation with a sufficient return contract may establish the effect directly; do not require redundant observation.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** A defined desired effect, a credible observation or return contract and a consumer of the result.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Executor, completion reporter and next action selector

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A defined desired effect, a credible observation or return contract and a consumer of the result.

**Observable postcondition.** The system’s completion claim matches the strongest established consequence rather than its own action request.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Independent observations can also fail; a missing acknowledgement does not prove no effect, making retries hazardous.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C005; EAA-C006

**Source ids.** EAA-S022; EAA-S033

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Independent observations can also fail; a missing acknowledgement does not prove no effect, making retries hazardous.; Introduced cost or harm: Observation cost, latency and possible duplicate side effects if uncertainty is mishandled.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Independent observations can also fail; a missing acknowledgement does not prove no effect, making retries hazardous.

**Source ids.** EAA-S022; EAA-S033

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** What establishes the intended change, and what does the current acknowledgement actually guarantee?; Observation sufficiency is task-specific; transport acknowledgement and application effect are not universally equivalent.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain evidence-proportionate completion semantics and explicitly unresolved outcomes, not ritual double-checking of every operation.

**Condition.** Actions with asynchronous, fallible, delayed or externally mediated effects.

**Limit.** Independent observations can also fail; a missing acknowledgement does not prove no effect, making retries hazardous.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

## EAA-024 — Bounded contingency handling and plan repair

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-024

**Native name.** Bounded contingency handling and plan repair

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/23

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** A repair or contingency policy after a failed or invalidated action or plan.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Across the remaining task budget, with explicit bounds on repeated attempts and escalation.

**Applicability guard.** The original route failed but the task may remain achievable within its authority and resource envelope.

**Material premises.** Failure evidence, alternative capabilities and irreversible side effects are considered.

**Evaluation rule.** Choose a bounded response appropriate to the failure, preserving the goal and protected conditions or openly revising the task through authorized control.

**Constructed positive case.** A transient read timeout permits a limited retry, while a confirmed invalid destination triggers clarification.

**Constructed failure witness.** The agent repeats the same invalid command indefinitely, or broadens permissions to force success.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** Stop with an accurate failure report when no justified repair exists; a fixed error handler can be adequate.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. Reactive execution and DS1 demonstrate contingency handling; ReAct and Reflexion supply modern bounded patterns. Reflexion’s negative task results defeat a universal retry-and-reflect prescription.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S016; EAA-S022; EAA-S030; EAA-S031; EAA-S039

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Alternative reactive methods, monitoring, contingency choice and plan revision.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S016

**Date.** 1987

**Work.** An Investigation into Reactive Planning in Complex Domains

**Version.** AAAI 1987 pp.202–206

**Source id.** EAA-S022

**Date.** 1999

**Work.** Validating the DS1 Remote Agent Experiment

**Version.** ISAIRAS 1999 primary experiment report

**Source id.** EAA-S030

**Date.** 2023

**Work.** ReAct: Synergizing Reasoning and Acting in Language Models

**Version.** arXiv:2210.03629v3; initial submission 2022; ICLR 2023 work

**Source id.** EAA-S031

**Date.** 2023-10-10

**Work.** Reflexion: Language Agents with Verbal Reinforcement Learning

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Mechanism.** Use observed failure to choose an applicable alternative, repair the plan or hand back; bound repetition by progress evidence, remaining resources and irreversible consequences.

**Trigger or context.** Execution failure, changed conditions or a detected unmet effect.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use a known safe retry for a transient failure when its conditions are established; stop directly when no useful recovery exists.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** Failure information, alternative capabilities or an exit path, and preservation of goal and authority constraints.

**Property ids.** EAA-022; EAA-023; EAA-026; EAA-069

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Executor, planner and supervising participant

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Failure information, alternative capabilities or an exit path, and preservation of goal and authority constraints.

**Observable postcondition.** Recovery either produces new progress evidence or terminates with an accurate unresolved/failure status.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Reflection can repeat the same error; a failure may stem from a wrong goal, sensor or oracle rather than the chosen action.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C006

**Source ids.** EAA-S020; EAA-S031; EAA-S039

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Reflection can repeat the same error; a failure may stem from a wrong goal, sensor or oracle rather than the chosen action.; Introduced cost or harm: Delay, additional actions, accumulated side effects and repair complexity.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Reflection can repeat the same error; a failure may stem from a wrong goal, sensor or oracle rather than the chosen action.

**Source ids.** EAA-S020; EAA-S031; EAA-S039

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** What new evidence makes another attempt worthwhile, and what stops ineffective recovery?; Retry safety and failure attribution are material; the environment may not reset and compensation may not restore the original state.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain failure-sensitive repair with explicit stopping and escalation, not retry count as a proxy for persistence or competence.

**Condition.** Execution failure, changed conditions or a detected unmet effect.

**Limit.** Reflection can repeat the same error; a failure may stem from a wrong goal, sensor or oracle rather than the chosen action.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

## EAA-025 — Irreversibility and retry/compensation boundary

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-025

**Native name.** Irreversibility and retry/compensation boundary

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/24

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** Repeated or compensating actions whose effects may persist after failure or uncertainty.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Across the entire attempt history, including partial completion and compensation.

**Applicability guard.** Retry can duplicate a side effect or reversal can be incomplete or costly.

**Material premises.** The action’s idempotency, transaction semantics or uncertainty about them is known at relevant scope.

**Evaluation rule.** Before retrying, distinguish no effect from unknown effect and choose deduplication, observation, compensation or escalation accordingly. Do not describe compensation as erasing history.

**Constructed positive case.** A payment retry uses the same idempotency key after a lost response.

**Constructed failure witness.** An agent retries a successful charge because the acknowledgement was lost, causing a second charge; refunding does not undo all user burden.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A pure read or idempotent state-setting action can use a simpler retry policy.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain as an analytical boundary grounded in action side effects and human-autonomy concerns. The cited agent literature does not establish a universal transaction protocol; this is a scoped synthesis, not an attributed theorem.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S022; EAA-S043; EAA-S044

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Action-specific human control considering reversibility, combined analytically with acknowledgement ambiguity.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S044

**Date.** 1993

**Work.** Learning Interface Agents

**Version.** AAAI 1993, pp.459–465

**Source id.** EAA-S043

**Date.** 1997

**Work.** Software Agents and User Autonomy

**Version.** Proceedings of Autonomous Agents 1997, pp.466–469

**Source id.** EAA-S022

**Date.** 1999

**Work.** Validating the DS1 Remote Agent Experiment

**Version.** ISAIRAS 1999 primary experiment report

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Mechanism.** Distinguish reversible, compensable, idempotent and irreversible actions where relevant; choose confirmation, evidence and recovery proportional to the actual effect, not just the command interface.

**Trigger or context.** External messages, resource spending, physical movement, destructive updates or uncertain action completion.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A harmless idempotent action may be retried without additional ceremony when that property is established.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** Known effect semantics, credible completion evidence and authority for any compensating action.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Executor and authorising participant

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Known effect semantics, credible completion evidence and authority for any compensating action.

**Observable postcondition.** An uncertain irreversible effect does not trigger a blind duplicate; compensation is described as a new action with its own residual consequences.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** The agent tradition does not supply a universal distributed-transaction guarantee; a user-approved action can still harm third parties.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C006

**Source ids.** EAA-S022; EAA-S043

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** The agent tradition does not supply a universal distributed-transaction guarantee; a user-approved action can still harm third parties.; Introduced cost or harm: Conservative delays, confirmation burden and incomplete restoration.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** The agent tradition does not supply a universal distributed-transaction guarantee; a user-approved action can still harm third parties.

**Source ids.** EAA-S022; EAA-S043

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** What happens if this command already took effect, and can the proposed recovery actually restore what matters?; Idempotence and reversibility must be demonstrated for the operation and environment; neither follows from a button labelled undo.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain consequence-sensitive execution and recovery as analytical composition of the sources, not a newly claimed native transaction theory.

**Condition.** External messages, resource spending, physical movement, destructive updates or uncertain action completion.

**Limit.** The agent tradition does not supply a universal distributed-transaction guarantee; a user-approved action can still harm third parties.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

## EAA-026 — Explicit time and resource envelope

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-026

**Native name.** Explicit time and resource envelope

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/25

**Original status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Selected status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Bearer and scope.** The resources consumed by an agent’s entire task execution and reasoning.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Over the accepted task and its remaining budget, with explicit units for measured time, money, calls or energy.

**Applicability guard.** Reasoning, acting or interaction can exceed available resources or defeat the task’s value.

**Material premises.** Material resource categories are identified; unavailable costs remain unmeasured rather than zero.

**Evaluation rule.** Choose and stop actions consistently with an explicit envelope, counting retries, evaluation and user effort where material.

**Constructed positive case.** A bounded search stops with its best adequate candidate before the deadline.

**Constructed failure witness.** A low API-price agent requires hours of human correction and is called cheap solely from token charges.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A simple hard time limit and fixed call cap may be enough; no optimizer is mandatory.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. Simon and bounded-optimality work establish why computation is part of rational choice. AI Agents That Matter measures important direct costs but cannot by itself establish full social or workflow cost.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S013; EAA-S014; EAA-S035

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Aspiration/resource-relative reasoning and machine/environment-relative bounded optimality.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S013

**Date.** 1955-02

**Work.** A Behavioral Model of Rational Choice

**Version.** Quarterly Journal of Economics 69(1), 99–118; CMU archival scan

**Source id.** EAA-S014

**Date.** 1995-05

**Work.** Provably Bounded-Optimal Agents

**Version.** JAIR 2, 575–609

**Source id.** EAA-S035

**Date.** 2024-07-01

**Work.** AI Agents That Matter

**Version.** arXiv:2407.01502v1

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Mechanism.** Include deliberation, sensing, retries and coordination in the operative resource envelope; let remaining resources constrain action and continuation decisions.

**Trigger or context.** Finite deadlines, paid computation/tools, limited energy or human attention.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A simple fixed bound suffices for predictable bounded tasks; no optimiser is required merely to enforce a cap.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** Measurable or conservatively bounded costs, a decision horizon and an executable response to exhaustion.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Deliberation scheduler, delegator and evaluator

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Measurable or conservatively bounded costs, a decision horizon and an executable response to exhaustion.

**Observable postcondition.** The agent does not treat reasoning or assistance as free, and can stop or fall back before exceeding the declared envelope.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Budget estimates may omit setup, human repair or retained infrastructure; a cheaper failed action may be worse overall.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C003

**Source ids.** EAA-S035

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Budget estimates may omit setup, human repair or retained infrastructure; a cheaper failed action may be worse overall.; Introduced cost or harm: Metering overhead and underuse of resources if limits are too conservative.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Budget estimates may omit setup, human repair or retained infrastructure; a cheaper failed action may be worse overall.

**Source ids.** EAA-S035

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** Which resources can this task consume, including reasoning and repair, and what enforces the limit?; Bounded optimality is machine/environment relative; cost weights are task commitments, not universal constants.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain explicit whole-task constraints while comparing quality and costs separately instead of collapsing them into one misleading score.

**Condition.** Finite deadlines, paid computation/tools, limited energy or human attention.

**Limit.** Budget estimates may omit setup, human repair or retained infrastructure; a cheaper failed action may be worse overall.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

## EAA-027 — Anytime response or adequate deadline fallback

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-027

**Native name.** Anytime response or adequate deadline fallback

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/26

**Original status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Selected status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Bearer and scope.** An anytime computation or fallback that must provide useful behavior before a deadline.

**Quantifier and horizon.** For the declared deadlines and admissible interruption times; a formal anytime guarantee is relative to its stated model.

**Applicability guard.** Computation can be interrupted or a delayed result would be unusable.

**Material premises.** The interim result or fallback meets an explicit adequacy threshold and switching has bounded overhead.

**Evaluation rule.** Establish what can be returned at each relevant deadline and whether it remains executable in the current state.

**Constructed positive case.** A route planner maintains a feasible incumbent and returns it when its budget expires.

**Constructed failure witness.** An algorithm calls itself anytime but produces no valid action until its final unbounded search step.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A constant-time rule is preferable when it meets the requirement; if no deadline exists, anytime machinery may add no value.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain conditionally. Bounded-optimality and architecture work motivate deadline-sensitive control, but an arbitrary intermediate language-model answer is not an adequate fallback merely because it exists.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S014; EAA-S046

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.
