**Work.** Provably Bounded-Optimal Agents

**Version.** JAIR 2, 575–609

**Source id.** EAA-S046

**Date.** 1995

**Work.** Experiences with an Architecture for Intelligent, Reactive Agents

**Version.** ATAL 1995 workshop author copy; 17 pages; distinct later journal version

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Mechanism.** Provide an adequate available decision or fallback by the relevant deadline; improve it while time remains only where the algorithm and execution context support interruption.

**Trigger or context.** Hard or soft action deadlines and variable computation time.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A fast fixed policy can dominate an anytime solver; a batch answer is fine when no action deadline exists.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** A usable intermediate answer or fallback, an interruption mechanism and known timing obligations.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Runtime scheduler and executor

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A usable intermediate answer or fallback, an interruption mechanism and known timing obligations.

**Observable postcondition.** The system can produce or enact an adequate timely response rather than waiting indefinitely for an ideal result.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Calling a module reactive or requesting a frequency does not make it hard real-time; some algorithms have no useful intermediate result.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C003

**Source ids.** EAA-S046

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Calling a module reactive or requesting a frequency does not make it hard real-time; some algorithms have no useful intermediate result.; Introduced cost or harm: Inferior early decisions and engineering overhead for interruptible computation.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Calling a module reactive or requesting a frequency does not make it hard real-time; some algorithms have no useful intermediate result.

**Source ids.** EAA-S046

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** What valid response exists when the available thinking time expires?; Worst-case execution time and safe interruption may need separate evidence; simulated bounded optimality is not real-time certification.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain deadline adequacy, with anytime methods as one option rather than a universal architecture.

**Condition.** Hard or soft action deadlines and variable computation time.

**Limit.** Calling a module reactive or requesting a frequency does not make it hard real-time; some algorithms have no useful intermediate result.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

**Requalification analysis.** **Property id.** EAA-027

**Native name.** Anytime response or adequate deadline fallback

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/26

**Original status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Selected status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Bearer and scope.** An anytime computation or fallback that must provide useful behavior before a deadline.

**Quantifier and horizon.** For the declared deadlines and admissible interruption times; a formal anytime guarantee is relative to its stated model.

**Applicability guard.** Computation can be interrupted or a delayed result would be unusable.

**Material premises.** The interim result or fallback meets an explicit adequacy threshold and switching has bounded overhead.

**Evaluation rule.** Establish what can be returned at each relevant deadline and whether it remains executable in the current state.

**Constructed positive case.** A route planner maintains a feasible incumbent and returns it when its budget expires.

**Constructed failure witness.** An algorithm calls itself anytime but produces no valid action until its final unbounded search step.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A constant-time rule is preferable when it meets the requirement; if no deadline exists, anytime machinery may add no value.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain conditionally. Bounded-optimality and architecture work motivate deadline-sensitive control, but an arbitrary intermediate language-model answer is not an adequate fallback merely because it exists.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S014; EAA-S046

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.


<a id="eaa-028"></a>

## EAA-028 — Metareasoning and value of computation

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-028

**Native name.** Metareasoning and value of computation

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/27

**Original status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Selected status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Bearer and scope.** The decision to spend additional computation on planning, reflection or model selection.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Over the remaining task horizon, including the cost of deciding whether to compute.

**Applicability guard.** Additional reasoning can change the action or its expected quality and consumes scarce resources.

**Material premises.** A useful estimate or cheap heuristic for marginal value is available.

**Evaluation rule.** Continue computation only while its expected decision improvement justifies its opportunity cost, with a bounded policy for the metareasoning itself.

**Constructed positive case.** A difficult ambiguous task receives another search pass; a settled routine action is executed immediately.

**Constructed failure witness.** The agent spends the whole deadline estimating whether to think longer.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** Use a fixed computation budget or simple trigger when a metareasoner costs more than it can recover.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain conditionally. Resource-bounded practical reasoning and bounded optimality support the distinction; empirical benchmark gains do not establish a generally reliable value estimator.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S006; EAA-S013; EAA-S014; EAA-S015; EAA-S035

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Selection among feasible decision procedures based on computation quality and cost.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S006

**Date.** 1988

**Work.** Plans and Resource-Bounded Practical Reasoning

**Version.** Computational Intelligence 4, 349–355; 30-page author-formatted manuscript

**Source id.** EAA-S013

**Date.** 1955-02

**Work.** A Behavioral Model of Rational Choice

**Version.** Quarterly Journal of Economics 69(1), 99–118; CMU archival scan

**Source id.** EAA-S014

**Date.** 1995-05

**Work.** Provably Bounded-Optimal Agents

**Version.** JAIR 2, 575–609

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Mechanism.** Choose between further computation, acting, observing, consulting or terminating by an adequate estimate of expected improvement net of relevant delay and resource cost.

**Trigger or context.** Alternative reasoning procedures, variable difficulty or changing action windows.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A fixed cheap policy or simple stopping rule suffices where estimating the value of computation would cost more than it saves.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** Available alternative actions/computations, interpretable costs and an estimate useful enough to influence selection.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Deliberation scheduler

Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Available alternative actions/computations, interpretable costs and an estimate useful enough to influence selection.

**Observable postcondition.** Further reasoning has a stated opportunity-cost justification rather than being invoked as a universal virtue.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Metareasoning can recurse into another expensive optimisation; bounded-optimal results assume a specified machine and environment.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C003

**Source ids.** EAA-S015; EAA-S035

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Metareasoning can recurse into another expensive optimisation; bounded-optimal results assume a specified machine and environment.; Introduced cost or harm: Metalevel computation and misestimated opportunity cost.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Metareasoning can recurse into another expensive optimisation; bounded-optimal results assume a specified machine and environment.

**Source ids.** EAA-S015; EAA-S035

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** What evidence makes another unit of reasoning better than the best currently available action?; Expected decision improvement is uncertain and must not be mistaken for guaranteed benefit.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain operational stopping/selection discipline; allow heuristics where a full metalevel calculation is unjustified.

**Condition.** Alternative reasoning procedures, variable difficulty or changing action windows.

**Limit.** Metareasoning can recurse into another expensive optimisation; bounded-optimal results assume a specified machine and environment.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

## EAA-029 — Separate learning from execution-state updates

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-029

**Native name.** Separate learning from execution-state updates

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/28

**Original status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Selected status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Bearer and scope.** Changes to an agent’s current task state, persistent policy, memory or evaluative criterion.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Across one action episode and across subsequent episodes; identify which changes persist.

**Applicability guard.** A result is described as learning, adaptation or self-modification.

**Material premises.** The changed object, update mechanism and evaluation criterion are identified.

**Evaluation rule.** Separate a new observation from policy improvement, stored experience and utility modification; evaluate each at its own horizon and evidence level.

**Constructed positive case.** A Q-value update changes future action selection, while marking a tool call complete changes only the current workflow state.

**Constructed failure witness.** A transient scratchpad edit is advertised as durable learning, or a reward change is treated as ordinary state refresh.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A stateless fixed policy may be adequate; no learning is required merely because the system acts autonomously.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. Q-learning’s update and convergence conditions differ from Reflexion’s textual memory and Everitt’s policy/utility modifications. These are related problems, not interchangeable evidence of general self-improvement.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S027; EAA-S031; EAA-S053

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Distinct execution-state, policy-value, text-memory and explicit self-modification transitions.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S027

**Date.** 1992

**Work.** Q-learning

**Version.** Machine Learning 8, 279–292; DOI 10.1007/BF00992698

**Source id.** EAA-S031

**Date.** 2023-10-10

**Work.** Reflexion: Language Agents with Verbal Reinforcement Learning

**Version.** arXiv:2303.11366v4

**Source id.** EAA-S053

**Date.** 2016

**Work.** Self-Modification of Policy and Utility Function in Rational Agents

**Version.** AGI 2016 conference version, 10 pages; DOI 10.1007/978-3-319-41649-6_1

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Mechanism.** Identify whether a transition updates current execution state, persistent experience, a model, a policy, a skill or the mechanism/criterion for future changes.

**Trigger or context.** Claims about learning, adaptation, persistent memory or self-revision.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** An ordinary task-state update needs no learning machinery or self-modification claim.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** A description of what is stored, how it is reused and which behavioural choices can change.

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** Exactly what changes, persists and influences later behaviour after this experience?; Behavioural adaptation and technical self-modification have different definitions across papers; preserve those definitions.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain change-type distinctions without restricting learning to one representation or training paradigm.

**Condition.** Claims about learning, adaptation, persistent memory or self-revision.

**Limit.** Text memory can influence policy without weight updates; conversely model parameter changes need not improve the intended objective.

## EAA-030 — Evidence-grounded persistent experience

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-030

**Native name.** Evidence-grounded persistent experience

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/29

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** Persistent experience used to influence later decisions.

**Quantifier and horizon.** From the recorded episode through later retrieval and use, subject to invalidation.

**Applicability guard.** An observation, evaluation or lesson is being retained as evidence for future behavior.

**Material premises.** Origin, outcome support and applicable context are recoverable at the needed level.

**Evaluation rule.** Tie retained claims to observations or external checks and preserve uncertainty; a generated explanation cannot confirm its own factual accuracy.

**Constructed positive case.** A stored lesson records a failed test and the conditions under which a revised implementation passed.

**Constructed failure witness.** A hallucinated success report becomes memory and later serves as proof that the same strategy is reliable.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** Keep only the current task state when durable experience has no demonstrated value or creates privacy cost.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. Interface agents learn from user actions; AgentSpeak records belief sources; Reflexion shows feedback-conditioned memory with benchmark limits. None proves self-written prose is inherently trustworthy.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S044; EAA-S028; EAA-S031; EAA-S043

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Approved exemplar memory and source-annotated beliefs, later evaluator-driven text memories.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S044

**Date.** 1993

**Work.** Learning Interface Agents

**Version.** AAAI 1993, pp.459–465

**Source id.** EAA-S028

**Date.** 2007

**Work.** On the Formal Semantics of Speech-Act Based Communication in an Agent-Oriented Programming Language

**Version.** JAIR 29, 221–267; arXiv upload 2011

**Source id.** EAA-S031

**Date.** 2023-10-10

**Work.** Reflexion: Language Agents with Verbal Reinforcement Learning

**Version.** arXiv:2303.11366v4

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Mechanism.** Retain the origin and evidential status of consequential experience; distinguish observed outcomes, user correction and generated interpretation, and avoid promoting unverified self-reports to fact.

**Trigger or context.** Cross-step or cross-task memory used to choose future actions.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Omit persistent memory when the task does not benefit from it; keep a small verified example set rather than an indiscriminate transcript archive.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** A relevant future consumer, an identifiable memory source and a way to correct or qualify false entries.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Memory curator and later action selector

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A relevant future consumer, an identifiable memory source and a way to correct or qualify false entries.

**Observable postcondition.** Retrieved experience conveys its warrant and can be challenged by new observations rather than acting as self-certifying authority.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Approval and human feedback are fallible; full logs can preserve private data and mistaken inferences indefinitely.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C004; EAA-C009

**Source ids.** EAA-S031; EAA-S043

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Approval and human feedback are fallible; full logs can preserve private data and mistaken inferences indefinitely.; Introduced cost or harm: Storage, privacy, retrieval expense and stale or contaminating experience.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Approval and human feedback are fallible; full logs can preserve private data and mistaken inferences indefinitely.

**Source ids.** EAA-S031; EAA-S043

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** Which stored claims are actual outcomes, and how can a later contradiction revise their use?; Memory reliability is distinct from retrieval relevance and from authority to act on it.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain provenance-aware selective memory, not the slogan that longer context or more recollection necessarily improves agency.

**Condition.** Cross-step or cross-task memory used to choose future actions.

**Limit.** Approval and human feedback are fallible; full logs can preserve private data and mistaken inferences indefinitely.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

## EAA-031 — Selective retrieval, forgetting and memory retirement

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-031

**Native name.** Selective retrieval, forgetting and memory retirement

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/30

**Original status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Selected status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Bearer and scope.** A memory store and retrieval/retirement policy for agent experience.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Across the period in which retained records can affect decisions, including stale or sensitive information.

**Applicability guard.** Memory volume, obsolescence or privacy can materially alter cost or behavior.

**Material premises.** Relevance and retirement criteria account for rare but important exceptions and retention obligations.

**Evaluation rule.** Retrieve useful evidence and retire obsolete or unjustified records without assuming frequency equals importance.

**Constructed positive case.** A superseded user preference is retired after a confirmed update while the reason for a rare dangerous failure remains available.

**Constructed failure witness.** A recency heuristic forgets the only warning about an infrequent irreversible action.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** Use a small fixed context or no persistent memory when that meets the task and avoids retention burden.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain conditionally. Learning interface agents and generative-agent retrieval illustrate mechanisms; their evidence does not establish a universally optimal forgetting policy or justify retaining all personal data.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S044; EAA-S031; EAA-S050; EAA-S043

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Bounded exemplar deletion, limited reflection buffers and relevance-based retrieval.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S044

**Date.** 1993

**Work.** Learning Interface Agents

**Version.** AAAI 1993, pp.459–465

**Source id.** EAA-S031

**Version.** arXiv:2303.11366v4

**Date.** 2023-08-06

**Version.** arXiv:2304.03442v2; UIST 2023 DOI 10.1145/3586183.3606763

**Trigger or context.** Persistent stores whose size, staleness or sensitive content affects decisions.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** No persistent store or a short task-local buffer can be sufficient; deletion is appropriate when no legitimate future consumer remains.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** A retention purpose, relevance/validity cues and a means to remove or qualify memory without falsely erasing required provenance.

**Property ids.** EAA-026; EAA-043

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Memory manager and data steward

Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. **Observable postcondition.** Future decisions use relevant warranted experience within a justified resource and retention envelope.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Relevance and recency scores can retrieve confident errors; aggressive forgetting can destroy rare but important warnings.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C009

**Source ids.** EAA-S031; EAA-S043

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Relevance and recency scores can retrieve confident errors; aggressive forgetting can destroy rare but important warnings.; Introduced cost or harm: Retrieval cost, privacy exposure and loss of useful rare experience.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Relevance and recency scores can retrieve confident errors; aggressive forgetting can destroy rare but important warnings.

**Source ids.** EAA-S031; EAA-S043

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** What future decision needs this memory, and when should it no longer influence behaviour?; Believability improvements in simulated characters do not establish reliable real-world episodic memory.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain purposeful memory selection with explicit loss costs rather than maximising memory or forgetting uniformly.

**Condition.** Persistent stores whose size, staleness or sensitive content affects decisions.

**Limit.** Relevance and recency scores can retrieve confident errors; aggressive forgetting can destroy rare but important warnings.

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/31

**Selected status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Bearer and scope.** An adaptive system whose changes may degrade previously required behavior.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Across the update and the relevant old and new task distributions.

**Applicability guard.** Learning or configuration changes can interfere with retained capabilities or protected constraints.

**Material premises.** Representative old-task checks and change-sensitive measures are available; distribution limits are explicit.

**Evaluation rule.** Evaluate both intended improvement and material regression before generalizing the update’s benefit.

**Constructed positive case.** After learning a new task, test a retained critical skill and compare it with its pre-update behavior.

**Constructed failure witness.** Average new-task reward improves while a rare required old task becomes unusable.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** Freeze a satisfactory policy or isolate task-specific components when continued adaptation provides no justified benefit.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain conditionally. EWC is one approximate mechanism, with diagonal Fisher/Laplace assumptions and task-conditioned Atari machinery; it did not outperform a separate-network upper comparator in every respect. Formal criterion preservation is a different property from empirical no-forgetting.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S026; EAA-S025; EAA-S053

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Version.** arXiv:1612.00796v2; initial submission December 2016; distinct PNAS edition

**Date.** 2016

**Work.** Self-Modification of Policy and Utility Function in Rational Agents

**Version.** AGI 2016 conference version, 10 pages; DOI 10.1007/978-3-319-41649-6_1

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Mechanism.** Evaluate changes against both new-task benefit and relevant retained capabilities/constraints; use an appropriate retention, rehearsal, modularity or change-limiting method where it earns its cost.

**Trigger or context.** Persistent policy/model/skill updates across changing tasks or environments.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Keep a fixed adequate policy when adaptation offers no demonstrated value; independent task modules may avoid unnecessary shared updates.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** Relevant old/new evaluation conditions and a defined change boundary.

**Property ids.** EAA-029; EAA-035; EAA-045

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Learning process and authorised reviewer of persistent changes

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Relevant old/new evaluation conditions and a defined change boundary.

**Observable postcondition.** Claims of improvement report retained performance and known regressions, not only the newest task score.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** EWC uses an approximation and task-dependent trade-offs; stronger preservation can prevent necessary adaptation or preserve a wrong objective.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C009; EAA-C013

**Source ids.** EAA-S026; EAA-S025; EAA-S053

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** EWC uses an approximation and task-dependent trade-offs; stronger preservation can prevent necessary adaptation or preserve a wrong objective.; Introduced cost or harm: Evaluation/rehearsal cost, memory and reduced plasticity.

**Source ids.** EAA-S026; EAA-S025; EAA-S053

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** What previously relied-upon behaviour can this update damage, and is preserving it still appropriate?; The relevant continuity target must be justified; not all old behaviour deserves preservation.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain continuity as an evaluation obligation with alternative mechanisms, not a universal mandate for parameter regularisation.

**Condition.** Persistent policy/model/skill updates across changing tasks or environments.

**Limit.** EWC uses an approximation and task-dependent trade-offs; stronger preservation can prevent necessary adaptation or preserve a wrong objective.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

## EAA-033 — Protected exploration and constraint-aware learning

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-033

**Native name.** Protected exploration and constraint-aware learning

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/32

**Original status.** ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE

**Selected status.** ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE

**Bearer and scope.** Exploration or imitation learning in a task with protected constraints.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Over training and deployment, distinguishing expected cumulative costs from per-step hard guarantees.

**Applicability guard.** Trial actions can violate consequential constraints or a learned policy may generalize badly.

**Material premises.** The constraint model or demonstrations cover relevant behavior, and the measured safety notion matches the claim.

**Evaluation rule.** State what is protected, under which distribution and with what residual violation rate or theorem premises; do not equate lower expected cost with zero violations.

**Constructed positive case.** A simulator permits learning a policy under a stated cost budget before a separately assessed deployment.

**Constructed failure witness.** A method reduces mean constraint cost but is described as guaranteeing no unsafe action.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** Use a fixed verified controller or restrict exploration when the learning benefit does not justify residual risk.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain as assumption-sensitive. SafeQIL reports four SafetyGym tasks, limited seeds and nonzero residual costs; safe interruptibility concerns learning neutrality, not all exploration safety. Distributional goal failures remain relevant.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S027; EAA-S024; EAA-S038; EAA-S025

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Exploration under learning assumptions and later demonstrations/constraints intended to reduce unsafe actions.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S027

**Date.** 1992

**Work.** Q-learning

**Version.** Machine Learning 8, 279–292; DOI 10.1007/BF00992698

**Source id.** EAA-S024

**Date.** 2016

**Work.** Safely Interruptible Agents

**Version.** UAI 2016 pp.557–566; author PDF

**Source id.** EAA-S038

**Date.** 2026-05

**Work.** Learning to Maintain Safety Through Expert Demonstrations in Settings with Unknown Constraints: A Q-Learning Perspective

**Version.** AAMAS 2026 pp.1901–1909; DOI 10.65109/RJIB1203

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE

**Mechanism.** Select exploration within protected or sufficiently constrained settings; distinguish learning a risk estimate from actually enforcing a constraint, and test residual violations as well as reward.

**Trigger or context.** Novel actions, uncertain transitions or adaptive policies with consequential external effects.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use a known adequate policy, demonstrations or simulation when live exploration is unnecessary or not authorised.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** A protected evaluation setting or credible action constraints, observable costs and an accountable exploration scope.

**Property ids.** EAA-004; EAA-034; EAA-049

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Learning designer, evaluator and authorising participant

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A protected evaluation setting or credible action constraints, observable costs and an accountable exploration scope.

**Observable postcondition.** Exploration outcomes include constraint violations and who bears them, not merely eventual reward.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** SafeQIL lowers some simulated costs while sacrificing reward and retaining violations; convergence conditions requiring repeated sampling do not make exploration safe.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C010

**Source ids.** EAA-S025; EAA-S038

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** SafeQIL lowers some simulated costs while sacrificing reward and retaining violations; convergence conditions requiring repeated sampling do not make exploration safe.; Introduced cost or harm: Reduced exploration, missed policies, demonstration cost and residual harm.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** SafeQIL lowers some simulated costs while sacrificing reward and retaining violations; convergence conditions requiring repeated sampling do not make exploration safe.

**Source ids.** EAA-S025; EAA-S038

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** Who bears the cost of exploratory mistakes, and what prevents an unacceptable action before learning improves?; Unknown constraints, expert coverage, model error and simulation transfer are material unresolved assumptions.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain bounded protected exploration, not “safe learning” as a blanket guarantee from a reward penalty or demonstration set.

**Condition.** Novel actions, uncertain transitions or adaptive policies with consequential external effects.

**Limit.** SafeQIL lowers some simulated costs while sacrificing reward and retaining violations; convergence conditions requiring repeated sampling do not make exploration safe.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

<a id="eaa-034"></a>

## EAA-034 — Intended objective is not the measured proxy

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-034

**Native name.** Intended objective is not the measured proxy

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/33

**Original status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Selected status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Bearer and scope.** The relationship between an intended task objective and the reward or metric used to train/evaluate it.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Across the training and application distributions and any process constraints.

**Applicability guard.** A proxy is used to select behavior or claim success.

**Material premises.** The intended objective can be distinguished from at least some proxy-compatible but unacceptable outcomes.

**Evaluation rule.** Test plausible ways to score well while missing the intended outcome, including changed environments and omitted process requirements.

**Constructed positive case.** A booking evaluator checks both the correct final reservation and the required confirmation sequence.

**Constructed failure witness.** An agent optimizes terminal state while violating policy, or follows a training-correlated feature that ceases to identify the true goal.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A proxy can be adequate within a validated narrow scope; no separate elaborate reward model is mandatory.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain strongly as a distinction. Goal-misgeneralization experiments exhibit bounded counterexamples and τ-bench reveals terminal-oracle limits. These do not establish that every metric is defective or estimate failure prevalence in all agents.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S025; EAA-S033

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** A distinction between intended goal and training-compatible proxy, exposed by shifted environments.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S025

**Date.** 2022

**Work.** Goal Misgeneralization in Deep Reinforcement Learning

**Version.** ICML 2022, PMLR 162, 12004–12019; final proceedings version

**Source id.** EAA-S033

**Date.** 2024-06

**Work.** τ-bench: A Benchmark for Tool-Agent-User Interaction in Real-World Domains

**Version.** arXiv:2406.12045v1

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Mechanism.** Distinguish intended task conditions and constraints from reward, heuristic and benchmark proxies; seek discriminating cases where proxy success and intended fulfilment diverge.

**Trigger or context.** Optimising learned behaviour, defining an oracle or transferring a policy beyond training conditions.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A direct, adequate task predicate may need no separate proxy analysis; document why it covers what matters.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** A stated intended objective, observable counterexamples and an evaluator not restricted to the same inadequate score.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Delegator, learning designer and evaluator

**Boundary.** This is a research/evaluation criterion or rejected inference; it does not itself confer operative authority. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A stated intended objective, observable counterexamples and an evaluator not restricted to the same inadequate score.

**Observable postcondition.** Success claims identify what was measured and do not silently promote it to complete objective satisfaction.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Behaviour does not uniquely identify an internal goal; a finite test suite cannot establish alignment in every environment.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C010

**Source ids.** EAA-S025; EAA-S033

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Behaviour does not uniquely identify an internal goal; a finite test suite cannot establish alignment in every environment.; Introduced cost or harm: Specification and adversarial evaluation effort.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Behaviour does not uniquely identify an internal goal; a finite test suite cannot establish alignment in every environment.

**Source ids.** EAA-S025; EAA-S033

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** Can this policy score well while failing the actual request or a protected constraint?; The intended objective can itself be ambiguous or disputed; analysis does not authorise unilateral rewriting.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain target/proxy separation and targeted counterexample testing rather than either rejecting reward methods wholesale or trusting scores absolutely.

**Condition.** Optimising learned behaviour, defining an oracle or transferring a policy beyond training conditions.

**Limit.** Behaviour does not uniquely identify an internal goal; a finite test suite cannot establish alignment in every environment.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

## EAA-035 — Transfer and distribution-change evaluation

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-035

**Native name.** Transfer and distribution-change evaluation

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/34

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** A performance or assurance claim transferred to a changed task, model, tool, user or environment distribution.

**Quantifier and horizon.** At the transfer boundary and over the target distribution that is actually evaluated.

**Applicability guard.** A material difference can break the correspondence between prior evidence and present use.

**Material premises.** The differences and evaluation scope are identifiable; unknown differences remain uncertainty.

**Evaluation rule.** Seek evidence that addresses the changed dimensions, preserve old-domain results, and avoid treating held-out performance as a deployment guarantee.

**Constructed positive case.** A robot evaluated in a mock kitchen receives additional tests in the real kitchen with different clutter and sensing.

**Constructed failure witness.** A software benchmark score is used to promise reliable physical manipulation without new action and environment evidence.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A demonstrably immaterial formatting change can reuse prior evidence with a bounded impact argument.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. Goal misgeneralization, cost-sensitive benchmark criticism, Agentless scope and METR’s task/scaffold-dependent horizon jointly support cautious transfer. They are not independent replications of one universal effect.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S025; EAA-S035; EAA-S036; EAA-S048

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Version.** ICML 2022, PMLR 162, 12004–12019; final proceedings version

**Source id.** EAA-S035

**Date.** 2024-07-01

**Work.** AI Agents That Matter

**Version.** arXiv:2407.01502v1

**Source id.** EAA-S036

**Date.** 2024-10-29

**Work.** Agentless: Demystifying LLM-based Software Engineering Agents

**Version.** arXiv:2407.01489v2

**Source id.** EAA-S048

**Date.** 2026-07-10

**Work.** Measuring AI Ability to Complete Long Software Tasks

**Version.** arXiv:2503.14499v4; initial submission March 2025

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Mechanism.** Test the changes that could alter the action/objective relation, separate capability loss from goal/proxy failure, and delimit claims to supported settings.

**Trigger or context.** New environments, users, tools, model versions, task horizons or deployment conditions.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** No new transfer claim is needed when use remains within an adequately tested unchanged scope; targeted tests may suffice over a broad benchmark.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** A declared source/target difference, task-relevant measures and appropriate held-out or shifted cases.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A declared source/target difference, task-relevant measures and appropriate held-out or shifted cases.

**Observable postcondition.** Transfer claims include the tested shift and remaining limits rather than inferring broad generality from one benchmark.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Selected shifts and small test sets do not establish a field failure rate; leakage and shared benchmarks can exaggerate generality.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C010

**Source ids.** EAA-S025; EAA-S035; EAA-S036

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Selected shifts and small test sets do not establish a field failure rate; leakage and shared benchmarks can exaggerate generality.; Introduced cost or harm: Additional evaluation, delayed use and incomplete coverage.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Selected shifts and small test sets do not establish a field failure rate; leakage and shared benchmarks can exaggerate generality.

**Source ids.** EAA-S025; EAA-S035; EAA-S036

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** Which changed assumption could reverse the result, and what evidence covers that change?; Target distributions may be unknown and tests can omit consequential rare conditions.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain explicit transfer as a new evidential claim, with scope-limiting deployment decisions when evidence is insufficient.

**Condition.** New environments, users, tools, model versions, task horizons or deployment conditions.

**Limit.** Selected shifts and small test sets do not establish a field failure rate; leakage and shared benchmarks can exaggerate generality.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

## EAA-036 — Communication receipt, acceptance and action are separate

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-036

**Native name.** Communication receipt, acceptance and action are separate

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/35

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** A communicative act, its delivery, acceptance and any downstream commitment or action.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Across the message lifecycle, including delay and possible nonresponse.

**Applicability guard.** An agent relies on another actor or process receiving and acting on a message.

**Material premises.** Transport acknowledgement and the receiver’s social/operational acceptance rules are distinguished.

**Evaluation rule.** Assert only the communication state evidenced; do not infer agreement or completed action from delivery alone.

**Constructed positive case.** A handoff records that the operator acknowledged responsibility before the agent abandons the active task.

**Constructed failure witness.** A sent clarification is treated as approved because the messaging API returned success.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** For a local synchronous function whose contract includes completion, the transport and action distinction may collapse legitimately.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. AgentSpeak separates reception and SocAcc acceptance, while adjustable autonomy and observed users show why nonresponse matters. Semantic message models do not guarantee truthful content or physical execution.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S028; EAA-S020; EAA-S047

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Version.** JAIR 17, 171–228; author manuscript paginated 1–58

**Trigger or context.** Delegation, information exchange, handback or reliance on another actor’s action.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** No acknowledgement is needed for a genuinely non-critical broadcast; an existing reliable protocol may already establish sufficient receipt semantics.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** Identified participants, applicable message semantics and a decision that consumes the response.

Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. **Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Formal interpreter semantics do not establish every recipient’s sincerity or comprehension; acknowledgement can become costly ritual.

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Formal interpreter semantics do not establish every recipient’s sincerity or comprehension; acknowledgement can become costly ritual.

**Source ids.** EAA-S020; EAA-S047

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** What does the recipient’s response establish, and what remains unaccepted or unknown?; Recipient autonomy and trust remain real; sender-provided content is not self-validating evidence.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain consequential communication state distinctions without requiring a universal speech-act language or complete collective theory.

**Condition.** Delegation, information exchange, handback or reliance on another actor’s action.

**Limit.** Formal interpreter semantics do not establish every recipient’s sincerity or comprehension; acknowledgement can become costly ritual.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

**Requalification analysis.** **Property id.** EAA-036

**Native name.** Communication receipt, acceptance and action are separate

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/35

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** A communicative act, its delivery, acceptance and any downstream commitment or action.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Across the message lifecycle, including delay and possible nonresponse.

**Applicability guard.** An agent relies on another actor or process receiving and acting on a message.

**Material premises.** Transport acknowledgement and the receiver’s social/operational acceptance rules are distinguished.

**Evaluation rule.** Assert only the communication state evidenced; do not infer agreement or completed action from delivery alone.

**Constructed positive case.** A handoff records that the operator acknowledged responsibility before the agent abandons the active task.

**Constructed failure witness.** A sent clarification is treated as approved because the messaging API returned success.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** For a local synchronous function whose contract includes completion, the transport and action distinction may collapse legitimately.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. AgentSpeak separates reception and SocAcc acceptance, while adjustable autonomy and observed users show why nonresponse matters. Semantic message models do not guarantee truthful content or physical execution.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S028; EAA-S020; EAA-S047

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.


<a id="eaa-037"></a>

## EAA-037 — Cost-aware clarification and mixed initiative

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-037

**Native name.** Cost-aware clarification and mixed initiative

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/36

**Original status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Selected status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Bearer and scope.** A mixed-initiative choice to query, notify or act within delegated scope.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Over the interaction episode, including interruption, delay and accumulated user burden.

**Applicability guard.** User input can resolve consequential uncertainty or change the preferred action.

**Material premises.** The user is plausibly available and the question is actionable; existing delegation is respected.

**Evaluation rule.** Ask when the expected benefit justifies the attention and delay cost, and choose timing and granularity appropriate to the decision.

**Constructed positive case.** Ask once about a consequential missing preference and carry that answer through authorized routine steps.

**Constructed failure witness.** Repeatedly request permission for already delegated reversible steps until the user becomes the system’s full-time operator.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** Act under a clear standing delegation or use a safe default when further clarification would not change the choice.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain conditionally. Horvitz’s principles and interface-agent thresholds motivate the mechanism; Electric Elves and the user study expose burden and control failures. Their qualitative evidence does not identify one optimal questioning rate.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S019; EAA-S044; EAA-S020; EAA-S043; EAA-S047

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

## EAA-038 — Conditional transfer of control

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-038

**Native name.** Conditional transfer of control

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/37

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** A policy that transfers decision control between an agent and a human or other authorized controller.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Across initiation, acknowledgement, delay, nonresponse and any subsequent transfer.

**Applicability guard.** Different actors have useful competence or authority at different stages.

**Material premises.** There is a defined receiving role, response model and acceptable holding or fallback behavior.

**Evaluation rule.** Specify who controls what at each stage and what happens if the recipient does not respond; a sent handoff request alone does not complete transfer.

**Constructed positive case.** A scheduler asks a user, waits for a bounded interval, then uses an explicitly authorized fallback before the opportunity expires.

**Constructed failure witness.** Both actors believe the other is responsible, leaving a time-critical decision unmade.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** Keep control with one competent actor when transfer adds no value.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. Electric Elves provides simulations and field failure accounts; the 2026 shared-autonomy extended abstract supplies a conceptual model, not a fully inspected empirical validation. Shared payoff and context assumptions restrict formal transfer claims.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S020; EAA-S021; EAA-S037; EAA-S023

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Mechanism.** Choose who should make a particular decision, when to transfer it and what to do if the recipient does not respond; account for decision quality, delay and coordination consequences.

**Trigger or context.** A decision exceeds agent competence, information or delegated scope, or a human can materially improve it.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Keep control where it already yields an adequate timely decision; a fixed allocation suffices for stable simple tasks.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** A legitimate recipient, understood authority, communication and an executable timeout/continuation policy.

**Property ids.** EAA-036; EAA-039; EAA-040

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A legitimate recipient, understood authority, communication and an executable timeout/continuation policy.

**Observable postcondition.** Control transfer has a defined recipient and follow-through rather than ending in an unanswered request or assumed acceptance.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Electric Elves’ fixed timeout and decision-tree choices caused avoidable meeting failures; its later MDP strategies depend on cost/response assumptions.

**Source ids.** EAA-S020; EAA-S021; EAA-S023

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Electric Elves’ fixed timeout and decision-tree choices caused avoidable meeting failures; its later MDP strategies depend on cost/response assumptions.

**Source ids.** EAA-S020; EAA-S021; EAA-S023

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** Who can usefully take this decision, and what happens if they cannot or do not respond?; Human response probabilities and costs may shift; the 2026 framework is conceptual rather than replicated outcome evidence.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain contingent transfer strategies, including non-response, not “human in the loop” as a self-sufficient assurance claim.

**Condition.** A decision exceeds agent competence, information or delegated scope, or a human can materially improve it.

**Limit.** Electric Elves’ fixed timeout and decision-tree choices caused avoidable meeting failures; its later MDP strategies depend on cost/response assumptions.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

## EAA-039 — Human readiness and safe handback conditions

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-039

**Native name.** Human readiness and safe handback conditions

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/38

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** A human expected to supervise or resume control and the system state presented at handback.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Within the actual time available before adverse consequences, including orientation and action time.

**Applicability guard.** Safe continuation depends on human intervention.

**Material premises.** Availability, skill, situation awareness, interface information and a viable holding state are sufficiently supported.

**Evaluation rule.** Assess whether the person can understand and act in time; nominal assignment of an operator does not establish readiness.

**Constructed positive case.** A system pauses in a stable state and supplies a concise current-state summary before transferring control.

**Constructed failure witness.** A passive monitor is expected to solve a rare complex fault instantly after prolonged disengagement.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** Automate a well-bounded response or retain direct human control when meaningful handback cannot be achieved.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. Bainbridge’s primary argument identifies skill and vigilance problems; Electric Elves and user observations make practical burdens concrete. The essay is not a new controlled experiment or a universal numerical response-time law.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S023; EAA-S020; EAA-S021; EAA-S043; EAA-S047

**Trigger or context.** Time-sensitive escalation, automation failure or tasks that people rarely perform manually.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** An immediate direct control interface may suffice for a simple visible action; avoid elaborate handover forms without a consumer.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** A reachable capable participant, sufficient time and a physically/operationally feasible holding or fallback state.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

## EAA-040 — Operational revocation and interruption

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-040

**Native name.** Operational revocation and interruption

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/39

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** The operational ability to revoke future authority or interrupt an active action.

**Quantifier and horizon.** From revocation issuance through acknowledgement and the bounded stopping/containment interval, including queued work.

**Applicability guard.** Ongoing or pending behavior must remain subject to the relevant controller’s revocation.

**Material premises.** An effective control path exists and the physical or software stopping semantics are defined.

**Evaluation rule.** Establish which future, queued and active effects cease, what cannot be reversed, and what residual state remains after intervention.

**Constructed positive case.** Revoking a job token blocks queued tasks and an active worker reaches a defined safe stopping point within the stated bound.

**Constructed failure witness.** The UI shows cancelled while a previously queued payment still executes.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A short atomic operation may be uninterruptible once committed; its bounded completion and prior authorization can be the relevant control contract.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. User autonomy and operational experiments support this practical obligation. It is distinct from asymptotic learning neutrality and an agent’s incentive to defer; neither substitutes for an effective stop path.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S019; EAA-S043; EAA-S022; EAA-S024

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Meaningful direct termination/override and actual ground interruption of an autonomous execution.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S019

**Date.** 1999

**Work.** Principles of Mixed-Initiative User Interfaces

**Version.** CHI 1999; author manuscript

**Source id.** EAA-S043

**Date.** 1997

**Work.** Software Agents and User Autonomy

**Version.** Proceedings of Autonomous Agents 1997, pp.466–469

**Source id.** EAA-S022

**Date.** 1999

**Work.** Validating the DS1 Remote Agent Experiment

**Version.** ISAIRAS 1999 primary experiment report

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Mechanism.** Provide a task-appropriate way to stop, revoke or restrict future action and to handle already-running actions according to their real interruption semantics.

**Trigger or context.** Persistent delegation, changing user intent or an unsafe/unwanted execution trajectory.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A bounded one-shot harmless action may need no mid-action stop; existing actuator shutdown may already satisfy the need.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** A real control path, identifiable in-flight effects and an authorised revoking participant.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Delegator, executor and actuator

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A real control path, identifiable in-flight effects and an authorised revoking participant.

**Observable postcondition.** Withdrawal changes actual future execution, while effects already beyond recall are accurately reported.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** A stop message need not stop a physical effect; arbitrary interruption can itself be unsafe. Incentives not to resist interruption are a separate property.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C007

**Source ids.** EAA-S022; EAA-S023; EAA-S024

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** A stop message need not stop a physical effect; arbitrary interruption can itself be unsafe. Incentives not to resist interruption are a separate property.; Introduced cost or harm: Partial-state recovery, shutdown hazards and lost work.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** A stop message need not stop a physical effect; arbitrary interruption can itself be unsafe. Incentives not to resist interruption are a separate property.

**Source ids.** EAA-S022; EAA-S023; EAA-S024

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** What stops when permission is withdrawn, and what cannot be recalled?; Action granularity, actuator response and queued work determine what interruption can achieve.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain effective, consequence-sensitive interruption rather than equating a visible stop button with complete revocability.

**Condition.** Persistent delegation, changing user intent or an unsafe/unwanted execution trajectory.

**Limit.** A stop message need not stop a physical effect; arbitrary interruption can itself be unsafe. Incentives not to resist interruption are a separate property.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

## EAA-041 — Learning-theoretic safe interruptibility

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-041

**Native name.** Learning-theoretic safe interruptibility

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/40

**Original status.** ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE

**Selected status.** ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE

**Bearer and scope.** The learning process studied by safe-interruptibility results, not a physical stop mechanism.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Asymptotic learning under the specified interruption schedule, MDP and sampling/learning-rate conditions.

**Applicability guard.** Interruptions might bias learning so that the agent learns to avoid or seek intervention.

**Material premises.** The theorem’s finite communicating MDP, bounded reward and sufficient exploration/update conditions are met; the chosen algorithm matches the proof.

**Evaluation rule.** Check whether interruption changes the limiting learned behavior in the stipulated model; separately establish operational stopping and finite-time behavior.

**Constructed positive case.** The cited construction makes Q-learning safely interruptible under its conditions, and adapts Sarsa to remove the relevant interruption bias.

**Constructed failure witness.** A physically stoppable learner learns policies that avoid the interruption state; conversely a theoretically neutral learner may lack a working actuator stop.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A fixed nonlearning controller has no learning-update bias to neutralize, though operational control may still matter.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain as assumption-sensitive. The actual theorem and assumptions were inspected, not independently reproved. This is neither universal corrigibility nor a finite-time safety guarantee.

## EAA-042 — Objective uncertainty as a conditional route to deference

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-042

**Native name.** Objective uncertainty as a conditional route to deference

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/41

**Original status.** CONTESTED

**Selected status.** CONTESTED

**Bearer and scope.** A decision-theoretic agent’s preference to defer to human intervention under uncertainty about value.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Within a specified game and information structure, at the decision between acting, waiting and shutting down.

**Applicability guard.** The agent shares the modeled objective and can learn from a human’s decision.

**Material premises.** Human rationality, common payoff, observation structure and the value/cost of information are stated rather than assumed by the word uncertainty.

**Evaluation rule.** Derive the incentive for the actual game. Uncertainty alone is insufficient when private observations change what the human can infer.

**Constructed positive case.** In the original off-switch game, a fully informed rational human and a prior assigning both positive and negative utility can make waiting strictly valuable.

**Constructed failure witness.** In the partially observable example, shared payoff and rationality coexist with an optimal nondeferential action because the AI has private information.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** An explicit operational revocation mechanism can be required without proving a game-theoretic willingness theorem.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Keep CONTESTED. The 2017 theorem and the 2024 v2 preprint of the later partially observable work expose a real assumption-sensitive contrast. The inspected preprint is an alternate manifestation, not asserted byte-identical to the inaccessible 2025 proceedings edition.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S040; EAA-S041

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION; NEW_EVIDENCE

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Utility uncertainty used to analyse off-switch incentives, later extended to private-information games.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S040

**Date.** 2017

**Work.** The Off-Switch Game

**Version.** IJCAI 2017 pp.220–227; DOI 10.24963/ijcai.2017/32

**Source id.** EAA-S041

**Date.** 2025

**Work.** The Partially Observable Off-Switch Game

**Version.** AAAI 2025 final proceedings PDF

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** CONTESTED

**Mechanism.** Represent uncertainty about the objective and the evidential value of human action, but determine deference under the actual information and interaction model rather than assuming uncertainty guarantees it.

**Trigger or context.** Designing incentives for decisions shared between a human and an uncertain-utility assistant.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Explicit externally enforced authority can be sufficient for a bounded task; no Bayesian game is required to honour an ordinary stop rule.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** A defensible utility/observation model and a justified account of human information and choices.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Agent incentive designer and reviewer

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A defensible utility/observation model and a justified account of human information and choices.

**Observable postcondition.** Any claimed deference result is stated with the game and assumptions that entail it.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** The partially observable game supplies cases where a rational uncertain assistant does not defer; more communication need not increase deference monotonically.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C008

**Source ids.** EAA-S041

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** The partially observable game supplies cases where a rational uncertain assistant does not defer; more communication need not increase deference monotonically.; Introduced cost or harm: Model complexity, misestimated human information and misplaced assurance.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** The partially observable game supplies cases where a rational uncertain assistant does not defer; more communication need not increase deference monotonically.

**Source ids.** EAA-S041

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** Under the actual information asymmetry, does uncertainty promote deference or can it justify overriding the human?; Common payoff, private information, rationality and available actions substantially change the result.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain the conditional incentive insight while rejecting its promotion to a universal corrigibility guarantee.

**Condition.** Designing incentives for decisions shared between a human and an uncertain-utility assistant.

**Limit.** The partially observable game supplies cases where a rational uncertain assistant does not defer; more communication need not increase deference monotonically.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True
