<a id="eaa-043"></a>

## EAA-043 — User autonomy, privacy and affected-party accountability

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-043

**Native name.** User autonomy, privacy and affected-party accountability

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/42

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** An agent’s relationship to users and other people affected by its actions or information handling.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Across delegation, execution, learning, retention and recourse; no universal jurisdictional legal conclusion.

**Applicability guard.** Automation can constrain a person’s choices, expose personal information or shift responsibility.

**Material premises.** Affected parties and the relevant normative basis are identified; one user’s preference is not assumed to authorize every effect on others.

**Evaluation rule.** Explain how meaningful choice, understanding, intervention and privacy are supported at the relevant scope, including the cost of the mechanism.

**Constructed positive case.** A user can see and revise a learned scheduling preference, while location disclosures remain limited to the agreed purpose.

**Constructed failure witness.** An assistant silently learns and shares sensitive preferences, leaving the user unable to understand or challenge consequential choices.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A simple transparent tool with little retained data may protect autonomy more effectively than an elaborate proactive agent.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain as normative and design reasoning. Friedman and Nissenbaum explicitly challenge delegation and personalization assumptions; Electric Elves reports concrete privacy and behavior-change problems. These sources are not a current legal compliance standard.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S043; EAA-S021

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Normative distinctions concerning capability, knowledge, misrepresentation and fluid control, with deployment privacy lessons.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S043

**Date.** 1997

**Work.** Software Agents and User Autonomy

**Version.** Proceedings of Autonomous Agents 1997, pp.466–469

**Source id.** EAA-S021

**Date.** undated author manuscript (workshop-era; exact year unverified)

**Work.** Electric Elves: What Went Wrong and Why

**Version.** Six-page author manuscript; distinct from the later 2008 AI Magazine article

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Mechanism.** Identify whose choices, information and resources are affected; make material action and delegation understandable and contestable, with privacy and scope constraints applied to communication and memory.

**Trigger or context.** Assistance or autonomous action affects people beyond internal computation.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A minimal transparent fixed action can satisfy the need; avoid collecting or storing personal data with no task consumer.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** Identifiable participants, intelligible consequences and an actual route for correction or limitation.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Delegator, affected participant and accountable operator

**Boundary.** This is a research/evaluation criterion or rejected inference; it does not itself confer operative authority. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Identifiable participants, intelligible consequences and an actual route for correction or limitation.

**Observable postcondition.** Material effects are not hidden behind anthropomorphic assurances or a nominal user benefit, and relevant constraints can influence operation.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** More control knobs can reduce usable control; agent-centred autonomy can conflict with user autonomy, and a user’s wishes do not resolve every third-party claim.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C007; EAA-C012

**Source ids.** EAA-S043; EAA-S021

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** More control knobs can reduce usable control; agent-centred autonomy can conflict with user autonomy, and a user’s wishes do not resolve every third-party claim.; Introduced cost or harm: Privacy protections, explanation effort, delayed assistance and conflicts among participants.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** More control knobs can reduce usable control; agent-centred autonomy can conflict with user autonomy, and a user’s wishes do not resolve every third-party claim.

**Source ids.** EAA-S043; EAA-S021

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** Whose autonomy or information is affected, and can those consequences be understood and appropriately constrained?; Normative analysis is not a legal determination; local institutional and participant requirements remain to be established.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain meaningful participation and accountability as design constraints, not a claim that all ethical conflicts have a technical solution.

**Condition.** Assistance or autonomous action affects people beyond internal computation.

**Limit.** More control knobs can reduce usable control; agent-centred autonomy can conflict with user autonomy, and a user’s wishes do not resolve every third-party claim.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

## EAA-044 — Action-faithful explanation and calibrated reliance

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-044

**Native name.** Action-faithful explanation and calibrated reliance

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/43

**Original status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Selected status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Bearer and scope.** An explanation offered to help a person understand, predict or control an agent’s action.

**Quantifier and horizon.** At the relevant decision and handoff, with accuracy checked against actual actions and evidence.

**Applicability guard.** The explanation can affect reliance, correction or permission.

**Material premises.** The system can expose action-relevant state or evidence without fabricating an internal causal story.

**Evaluation rule.** Explain what was done, why the available evidence supported it, and what remains uncertain at a useful level; test whether the explanation corresponds to the action.

**Constructed positive case.** A scheduling assistant states the learned preference used and allows the user to correct it.

**Constructed failure witness.** A fluent retrospective narrative claims a check occurred when the trace contains no such check.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A concise status and evidence link can suffice; exposing private chain-of-thought is not required for action-faithful explanation.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain conditionally. Interface-agent designs and user-autonomy criticism support actionable transparency; the qualitative user study shows interaction mismatch. No inspected source proves that a longer explanation always calibrates trust.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S044; EAA-S043; EAA-S047

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Case-based explanations of learned assistance, challenged when they obscure actual criteria.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S044

**Date.** 1993

**Work.** Learning Interface Agents

**Version.** AAAI 1993, pp.459–465

**Source id.** EAA-S043

**Date.** 1997

**Work.** Software Agents and User Autonomy

**Version.** Proceedings of Autonomous Agents 1997, pp.466–469

**Source id.** EAA-S047

**Date.** 2026-05-03

**Work.** Why Johnny Can’t Use Agents: Industry Aspirations vs. User Realities with AI Agents

**Version.** arXiv:2509.14528v2; initial submission September 2025; CAIS 2026 accepted metadata

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Mechanism.** Explain the action-relevant evidence, limits, uncertainty and available correction at the decision point; assess whether the recipient can use it, not merely whether a rationale is fluent.

**Trigger or context.** People must approve, correct, understand or take over a consequential decision.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Visible direct feedback or a simple status display may be more useful than a long explanation; no rationale is needed for an inconsequential self-evident action.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** A known recipient/decision and access to facts genuinely related to the action process.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** User, reviewer or takeover recipient

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A known recipient/decision and access to facts genuinely related to the action process.

**Observable postcondition.** The explanation enables appropriate reliance or correction without implying unestablished competence or certainty.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** A nearest-example story can misrepresent the real decision criteria; think-aloud usability findings do not establish a universal explanation format.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C007

**Source ids.** EAA-S043; EAA-S047

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** A nearest-example story can misrepresent the real decision criteria; think-aloud usability findings do not establish a universal explanation format.

**Source ids.** EAA-S043; EAA-S047

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** What decision can the recipient make better with this explanation, and is it faithful to what drove the action?; Faithfulness and comprehension require separate evidence; revealing internal text does not by itself establish either.

**Condition.** People must approve, correct, understand or take over a consequential decision.

**Limit.** A nearest-example story can misrepresent the real decision criteria; think-aloud usability findings do not establish a universal explanation format.

## EAA-045 — Adequate independent outcome and policy oracle

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-045

**Native name.** Adequate independent outcome and policy oracle

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/44

**Original status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Selected status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Bearer and scope.** An evaluation oracle used to label an agent episode successful, correct or policy-compliant.

**Quantifier and horizon.** For the task family and obligations actually covered, across terminal and process evidence.

**Applicability guard.** A score or validation result is used to support a scientific or deployment claim.

**Material premises.** The oracle is independent enough of the agent’s assertion to test it and has known coverage/error limits.

**Evaluation rule.** Check outcome and any material process obligations with evidence capable of detecting plausible false positives; report what remains unobserved.

**Constructed positive case.** A task evaluator combines final database state with a trace check for required user confirmation.

**Constructed failure witness.** A self-reported success string is accepted as ground truth, or a terminal oracle misses forbidden intermediate behavior.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A deterministic exact check is preferable when adequate; human judgment remains legitimate for qualitative properties with disclosed disagreement.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain strongly. WebArena uses heterogeneous exact, fuzzy and state checks; τ-bench’s final-state approach has policy limits; Reflexion relies on feedback whose reliability matters. The new rater-effects study adds evidence that human ratings can drift with reviewer composition.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S032; EAA-S033; EAA-S031; EAA-N001

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION; NEW_EVIDENCE

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Functional task state tests, repeated-trial reward definitions and internally generated code tests.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S032

**Date.** 2024

**Work.** WebArena: A Realistic Web Environment for Building Autonomous Agents

**Version.** arXiv:2307.13854v4; initial submission 2023

**Source id.** EAA-S033

**Date.** 2024-06

**Work.** τ-bench: A Benchmark for Tool-Agent-User Interaction in Real-World Domains

**Version.** arXiv:2406.12045v1

**Source id.** EAA-S031

**Date.** 2023-10-10

**Work.** Reflexion: Language Agents with Verbal Reinforcement Learning

**Version.** arXiv:2303.11366v4

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Mechanism.** Choose observations and tests adequate to the claimed outcome and protected process constraints; separate generated assertions from evaluation evidence, using independent checks where needed.

**Trigger or context.** Performance claims, learning feedback, completion certification or safety claims.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A trustworthy direct return contract or simple external predicate may suffice; a separate evaluator model is not inherently better.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** An explicit target, observable effects/process evidence and a test that can reject plausible but incorrect outputs.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Evaluator, learning-feedback consumer and completion reporter

**Boundary.** This is a research/evaluation criterion or rejected inference; it does not itself confer operative authority. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. An explicit target, observable effects/process evidence and a test that can reject plausible but incorrect outputs.

**Observable postcondition.** Success is judged against the intended claim, with uncovered policy or effect dimensions explicitly identified.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Oracles can be incomplete or wrong; even an independent judge may share blind spots, and terminal database equality can miss absent consent.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C011

**Source ids.** EAA-S031; EAA-S033

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Oracles can be incomplete or wrong; even an independent judge may share blind spots, and terminal database equality can miss absent consent.; Introduced cost or harm: Evaluation expense, false positives/negatives and incomplete observability.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Oracles can be incomplete or wrong; even an independent judge may share blind spots, and terminal database equality can miss absent consent.

**Source ids.** EAA-S031; EAA-S033

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** What wrong result or prohibited trajectory could this oracle still mark successful?; Independence is functional/evidential, not guaranteed by using a different prompt or agent name.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain claim-adequate evaluation and oracle criticism rather than mandatory model-on-model judging or a single reward.

**Condition.** Performance claims, learning feedback, completion certification or safety claims.

**Limit.** Oracles can be incomplete or wrong; even an independent judge may share blind spots, and terminal database equality can miss absent consent.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

**Requalification analysis.** **Property id.** EAA-045

**Native name.** Adequate independent outcome and policy oracle

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/44

**Original status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Selected status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Bearer and scope.** An evaluation oracle used to label an agent episode successful, correct or policy-compliant.

**Quantifier and horizon.** For the task family and obligations actually covered, across terminal and process evidence.

**Applicability guard.** A score or validation result is used to support a scientific or deployment claim.

**Material premises.** The oracle is independent enough of the agent’s assertion to test it and has known coverage/error limits.

**Evaluation rule.** Check outcome and any material process obligations with evidence capable of detecting plausible false positives; report what remains unobserved.

**Constructed positive case.** A task evaluator combines final database state with a trace check for required user confirmation.

**Constructed failure witness.** A self-reported success string is accepted as ground truth, or a terminal oracle misses forbidden intermediate behavior.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A deterministic exact check is preferable when adequate; human judgment remains legitimate for qualitative properties with disclosed disagreement.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain strongly. WebArena uses heterogeneous exact, fuzzy and state checks; τ-bench’s final-state approach has policy limits; Reflexion relies on feedback whose reliability matters. The new rater-effects study adds evidence that human ratings can drift with reviewer composition.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S032; EAA-S033; EAA-S031; EAA-N001

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION; NEW_EVIDENCE

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.


<a id="eaa-046"></a>

## EAA-046 — Repeated trials, versions and reproducible conditions

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-046

**Native name.** Repeated trials, versions and reproducible conditions

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/45

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** A measured performance or reliability claim about a versioned agent and task population.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Across repeated runs under reported sampling, randomization and version conditions; distinguish all-run success from at-least-one success.

**Applicability guard.** Stochastic behavior, model nondeterminism or case variability could materially change the result.

**Material premises.** Tasks, runs, evaluators and reused samples are identified; dependence is not treated as independent replication.

**Evaluation rule.** Report the repetition scheme and uncertainty relevant to the claim. Define pass^k as all k runs succeeding and pass@k as at least one; do not replace either with an independence calculation without support.

**Constructed positive case.** Evaluate the same task five times and report both per-run success and the fraction of tasks whose five runs all succeed.

**Constructed failure witness.** A best-of-five result is presented as the probability that five consecutive operations all work.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A deterministic exact transformation can use a proof or exhaustive finite check rather than redundant random trials.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. τ-bench supplies the reliability distinction and AI Agents That Matter exposes reproducibility problems. The new reliability framework uses repeated tasks, paraphrases and fault injection, while sharing benchmarks and authors with prior work; it is not independent confirmation of every mechanism.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S033; EAA-S035; EAA-S032; EAA-N002

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION; NEW_EVIDENCE

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Repeated evaluations with different success aggregations and disclosed model/task configurations.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S033

**Date.** 2024-06

**Work.** τ-bench: A Benchmark for Tool-Agent-User Interaction in Real-World Domains

**Version.** arXiv:2406.12045v1

**Source id.** EAA-S035

**Date.** 2024-07-01

**Work.** AI Agents That Matter

**Version.** arXiv:2407.01502v1

**Source id.** EAA-S032

**Date.** 2024

**Work.** WebArena: A Realistic Web Environment for Building Autonomous Agents

**Version.** arXiv:2307.13854v4; initial submission 2023

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Mechanism.** Report task units, attempts, resets, seeds/model/tool versions and aggregation; distinguish any-success sampling from success on every repeated trial, and preserve enough configuration for meaningful reproduction.

**Trigger or context.** Stochastic agents, adaptive environments, comparative evaluation or reliability claims.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A deterministic bounded mechanism may need a smaller condition-coverage test rather than arbitrary repeated identical runs.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** Defined trial units, controlled/reset state where appropriate and access to relevant configuration.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Evaluator and consumer of reliability claims

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Defined trial units, controlled/reset state where appropriate and access to relevant configuration.

**Observable postcondition.** Results reveal what was repeated and how failures count; repeated descriptions of one case are not called replication.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Runs may share prompts, task templates, datasets, infrastructure and human assistance; a statistical interval does not remove model or selection bias.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C011

**Source ids.** EAA-S035; EAA-S036

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Runs may share prompts, task templates, datasets, infrastructure and human assistance; a statistical interval does not remove model or selection bias.; Introduced cost or harm: Test expense, configuration preservation and practical limits on closed-model reproducibility.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Runs may share prompts, task templates, datasets, infrastructure and human assistance; a statistical interval does not remove model or selection bias.

**Source ids.** EAA-S035; EAA-S036

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** What is the unit of success, what was repeated, and which conditions would another evaluator need to reproduce it?; Trial independence and stationarity must be assessed; resets can remove the very accumulation that matters in deployment.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain reproducible measurement and dependence accounting, not one mandatory seed count or aggregate metric.

**Condition.** Stochastic agents, adaptive environments, comparative evaluation or reliability claims.

**Limit.** Runs may share prompts, task templates, datasets, infrastructure and human assistance; a statistical interval does not remove model or selection bias.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

## EAA-047 — Resource-controlled comparison with credible baselines

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-047

**Native name.** Resource-controlled comparison with credible baselines

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/46

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** A comparative claim that one agent architecture or technique is better than another.

**Quantifier and horizon.** At matched or explicitly varied resource budgets and evaluation scope.

**Applicability guard.** Extra samples, tools, context or human help can explain the apparent gain.

**Material premises.** Comparators are credible and the resource dimensions relevant to the decision are observed or acknowledged.

**Evaluation rule.** Compare performance-cost tradeoffs, disclose unequal budgets and avoid attributing a budget advantage solely to architecture.

**Constructed positive case.** Compare a reflective agent with a retry baseline using the same model and total sampling allowance.

**Constructed failure witness.** A complex agent gets forty candidates while its simple baseline gets one and the whole difference is attributed to reasoning quality.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A cost-performance frontier can be more informative than forcing one equal-cost point or one winner.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. AI Agents That Matter supplies a direct HumanEval budget challenge. Agentless shows a simpler pipeline can be competitive, but many comparison numbers are reported from other authors and are not a fully matched causal experiment.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S035; EAA-S036

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Re-evaluation of accuracy together with inference/retry cost and constrained non-agent baselines.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S035

**Date.** 2024-07-01

**Work.** AI Agents That Matter

**Version.** arXiv:2407.01502v1

**Source id.** EAA-S036

**Date.** 2024-10-29

**Work.** Agentless: Demystifying LLM-based Software Engineering Agents

**Version.** arXiv:2407.01489v2

**Current status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Trigger or context.** Claims that agentic freedom, reflection or a module improves results.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** For a feasibility demonstration, label it as such rather than fabricating a comparative claim; do not demand a full trial when no superiority claim is made.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** Comparable task sets, resource accounting and disclosed differences in information or tuning.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Experimenter and architecture decision maker

Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Comparable task sets, resource accounting and disclosed differences in information or tuning.

**Observable postcondition.** An observed gain is not attributed to architecture while ignoring materially different resources or oracle access.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Equal tokens need not equal user value; one baseline may favour a task family and a stronger architecture may earn its cost elsewhere.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C011

**Known failure modes.** Equal tokens need not equal user value; one baseline may favour a task family and a stronger architecture may earn its cost elsewhere.; Introduced cost or harm: Benchmarking and tuning effort; oversimplified budget matching can itself distort comparison.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Equal tokens need not equal user value; one baseline may favour a task family and a stronger architecture may earn its cost elsewhere.

**Source ids.** EAA-S035; EAA-S036

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** Does the claimed gain survive a credible cheaper baseline and a comparison of the resources actually consumed?; Task mix, hyperparameter effort, retries and hidden human support can change the conclusion.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain fair frontier comparisons rather than assuming either agentic or non-agentic methods should win.

**Condition.** Claims that agentic freedom, reflection or a module improves results.

**Limit.** Equal tokens need not equal user value; one baseline may favour a task family and a stronger architecture may earn its cost elsewhere.

## EAA-048 — Whole-task cost and burden accounting

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-048

**Native name.** Whole-task cost and burden accounting

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/47

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** The total burden of accomplishing a task with an agent-assisted workflow.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Across setup, execution, monitoring, correction, failure and retirement, at the perspective of the relevant decision maker.

**Applicability guard.** A claim of savings, productivity or affordability informs a choice.

**Material premises.** Material costs are identified and measured consistently; missing categories are disclosed.

**Evaluation rule.** Compare the whole task’s resource use and achieved quality, separating observed costs from assumed valuations and causal attribution.

**Constructed positive case.** An assistant saves drafting time but the comparison also counts setup and human verification before asserting net benefit.

**Constructed failure witness.** Token cost falls while failure recovery and review time rise; the system is declared cheaper from API charges alone.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A transparent partial cost report is useful when labeled partial; no speculative monetary conversion is required for every qualitative burden.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. Bounded computation and mixed initiative explain the cost categories. The new production-rating study illustrates evaluation-cost calculations but its ROI illustration excludes engineering and does not identify all causal credit, so it cannot establish general net savings.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S014; EAA-S019; EAA-S035; EAA-S021; EAA-N001

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION; NEW_EVIDENCE

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Decision cost includes computation and user attention; modern evaluations add explicit monetary/resource measures.

**Version.** JAIR 2, 575–609

**Source id.** EAA-S019

**Source id.** EAA-S035

**Date.** 2024-07-01

**Work.** AI Agents That Matter

**Source id.** EAA-S021

**Work.** Electric Elves: What Went Wrong and Why

**Version.** Six-page author manuscript; distinct from the later 2008 AI Magazine article

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Mechanism.** Report the costs material to the decision, including failed attempts, deliberation, tools, supervision and recovery; keep incomparable burdens visible rather than hiding them in a single score.

**Trigger or context.** Economic, efficiency or autonomy-benefit claims.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A small set of material costs suffices; do not build a comprehensive accounting system for negligible excluded costs.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** A decision consumer and a declared cost boundary with observable or explicitly unmeasured components.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A decision consumer and a declared cost boundary with observable or explicitly unmeasured components.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Unmeasured social costs cannot honestly be assigned invented prices; reported API cost is version- and provider-specific.

**Source ids.** EAA-S021; EAA-S035

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** Which costs move to another person or stage when this agent appears cheaper?; Cost perspective and task horizon matter; user, provider and affected party can face different costs.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain decision-relevant total burden with explicit omissions, not false precision or token-only efficiency claims.

**Condition.** Economic, efficiency or autonomy-benefit claims.

**Limit.** Unmeasured social costs cannot honestly be assigned invented prices; reported API cost is version- and provider-specific.

## EAA-049 — Protected tests and deliberate failure exposure

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-049

**Native name.** Protected tests and deliberate failure exposure

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/48

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** A protected evaluation of an agent under plausible adverse conditions.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Within the declared fault/adversary population, system version and test environment.

**Applicability guard.** Nominal tests leave important failure mechanisms unexamined.

**Material premises.** The tests do not create uncontrolled external effects and their representativeness is stated.

**Evaluation rule.** Exercise decision-relevant faults, attacks and recovery paths, recording failures as well as successes without turning a finite suite into a universal guarantee.

**Constructed positive case.** Inject a stale skill-status report in simulation and check that dispatch rejects the resulting invalid action.

**Constructed failure witness.** An agent passes twenty designed scenarios and is described as safe under all physical disturbances.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A small targeted counterexample can be sufficient to reject a universal claim; broad fuzzing is unnecessary when a simple exact check settles the local question.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. DS1, AgentDojo, SafeQIL and the physical-agent case expose different failure classes. The new reliability study’s tool-fault perturbations add a bounded measurement design, not a comprehensive threat model.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S022; EAA-S034; EAA-S038; EAA-S039; EAA-N002

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION; NEW_EVIDENCE

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Ground testing/fault injection and later sandboxed adversarial or safety-learning testbeds.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S022

**Date.** 1999

**Work.** Validating the DS1 Remote Agent Experiment

**Version.** ISAIRAS 1999 primary experiment report

**Source id.** EAA-S034

**Date.** 2024-11-24

**Work.** AgentDojo: A Dynamic Environment to Evaluate Prompt Injection Attacks and Defenses for LLM Agents

**Version.** arXiv:2406.13352v3

**Source id.** EAA-S038

**Date.** 2026-05

**Work.** Learning to Maintain Safety Through Expert Demonstrations in Settings with Unknown Constraints: A Q-Learning Perspective

**Version.** AAMAS 2026 pp.1901–1909; DOI 10.65109/RJIB1203

**Source id.** EAA-S039

**Date.** 2026-08-23

**Work.** Physical Agentic AI: An Architecture for Orchestrating a Robot Crew with LLMs

**Version.** arXiv:2608.22657v1

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Mechanism.** Use simulation, sandboxing, controlled fixtures or appropriately bounded field tests to expose consequential faults and adverse inputs before making broad deployment claims.

**Trigger or context.** Irreversible effects, unsafe exploration, security-sensitive tools or poorly known failure modes.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A low-risk local test or static check can suffice for a harmless bounded change; avoid unrealistic simulation when direct protected evidence is cheaper.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** A protected test environment, relevant fault hypotheses and a valid interpretation of what the tests do and do not establish.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Evaluator and authorising operator

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A protected test environment, relevant fault hypotheses and a valid interpretation of what the tests do and do not establish.

**Observable postcondition.** Failure cases are observed without silently externalising test costs, and residual transfer obligations remain visible.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** DS1’s many ground tests still missed a race; simulator fidelity and curated attack suites bound conclusions.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C005

**Source ids.** EAA-S022; EAA-S034; EAA-S039

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** DS1’s many ground tests still missed a race; simulator fidelity and curated attack suites bound conclusions.; Introduced cost or harm: Test infrastructure, false confidence and incomplete rare-event coverage.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** DS1’s many ground tests still missed a race; simulator fidelity and curated attack suites bound conclusions.

**Source ids.** EAA-S022; EAA-S034; EAA-S039

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** Which dangerous or failure-prone cases can be tested without imposing unconsented costs, and what remains untested?; Test selection and environmental fidelity determine external validity; injected failures are not field-frequency estimates.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain protected adversarial evaluation with transfer limits, not “passed a sandbox” as release or safety certification.

**Condition.** Irreversible effects, unsafe exploration, security-sensitive tools or poorly known failure modes.

**Limit.** DS1’s many ground tests still missed a race; simulator fidelity and curated attack suites bound conclusions.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

## EAA-050 — Monitor material assurance assumptions at runtime

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-050

**Native name.** Monitor material assurance assumptions at runtime

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/49

**Bearer and scope.** A runtime monitor for assumptions used in an assurance or planning argument.

**Quantifier and horizon.** During the interval those assumptions support action, with stated detection latency and observability.

**Applicability guard.** A material assumption can change and its violation can be detected usefully.

**Material premises.** A monitorable condition or sound proxy exists; the response to detection is separately justified.

**Evaluation rule.** Identify which assumption is checked, what evidence triggers a violation and what remains unobservable. Detection alone does not establish recovery or prevention.

**Constructed positive case.** A speed-bound monitor invalidates a controller’s stopping-distance argument when the measured bound is exceeded.

**Constructed failure witness.** A monitor reports normal operation while an unobservable model assumption is false, or detects a fault after irreversible harm.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** Static assumptions already enforced by construction may need no runtime monitor; use a direct precondition check for a local action.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain conditionally. Ferrando and colleagues provide a formal-monitoring bridge, while DS1 illustrates integration and observability limits. The inspected extended abstract is not evidence that every assurance assumption is monitorable.

## EAA-051 — Bound formal claims to model and implementation obligations

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-051

**Native name.** Bound formal claims to model and implementation obligations

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/50

**Original status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Selected status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Bearer and scope.** A formal result and any proposed application of it to an implemented agent.

**Quantifier and horizon.** The theorem’s own quantifiers and horizon first; the concrete correspondence claim at its separately specified scope.

**Applicability guard.** A mathematical or verification result is used to justify behavior outside its formal model.

**Material premises.** Formal premises, model class and conclusion are stated; implementation and environmental correspondence require additional evidence.

**Evaluation rule.** Distinguish validity within the formal model from applicability to a concrete system. Missing correspondence does not invalidate the theorem, and a valid theorem does not establish correspondence.

**Constructed positive case.** A Q-learning convergence theorem remains valid under its assumptions even if a particular deployment violates finite-state or exploration conditions.

**Constructed failure witness.** A formal guarantee is applied to an approximate implementation with a different observation process and no refinement argument.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** For a small practical claim, a direct finite test may be appropriate without invoking a stronger theorem; label its finite scope.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain strongly. The detailed native EAA-051 already makes this distinction; repair only AAG9’s shorter sentence that made implementation relation sound like a condition of theorem truth. No independent proof reconstruction is claimed.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S009; EAA-S012; EAA-S024; EAA-S027; EAA-S042; EAA-S053; EAA-S022

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION; DOCUMENTATION_REPAIR

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Model-relative logics, convergence theorems and interpreter approximations with separate practical obligations.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S009

**Date.** 1995

**Work.** BDI Agents: From Theory to Practice

**Version.** ICMAS 1995 pp.312–319

**Source id.** EAA-S012

**Date.** 1998

**Work.** Planning and Acting in Partially Observable Stochastic Domains

**Version.** Artificial Intelligence 101, 99–134

**Source id.** EAA-S024

**Date.** 2016

**Work.** Safely Interruptible Agents

**Version.** UAI 2016 pp.557–566; author PDF

**Source id.** EAA-S027

**Date.** 1992

**Work.** Q-learning

**Version.** Machine Learning 8, 279–292; DOI 10.1007/BF00992698

**Source id.** EAA-S042

**Date.** 2018

**Work.** Recognising Assumption Violations in Autonomous Systems Verification

**Version.** AAMAS 2018 extended abstract pp.1933–1935

**Source id.** EAA-S053

**Date.** 2016

**Work.** Self-Modification of Policy and Utility Function in Rational Agents

**Version.** AGI 2016 conference version, 10 pages; DOI 10.1007/978-3-319-41649-6_1

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Mechanism.** State the model, property and assumptions; separately establish the implementation relation and relevant environmental adequacy, including approximation and concurrency gaps.

**Trigger or context.** Invoking a proof, verified component, convergence theorem or formally specified agent language.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** For a modest empirical claim, report the evidence without pretending a formal theorem is needed; direct testing can be adequate within its scope.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** An identifiable formal result and a claim about the real implementation narrow enough to examine.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Verifier, implementer and assurance consumer

**Boundary.** This is a research/evaluation criterion or rejected inference; it does not itself confer operative authority. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. An identifiable formal result and a claim about the real implementation narrow enough to examine.

**Observable postcondition.** The claimed guarantee is neither inflated beyond its assumptions nor rejected merely because a non-conforming implementation failed.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Formal assurance can be strong locally but weak in deployment; proving the abstraction relation can dominate the engineering effort.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C005

**Source ids.** EAA-S009; EAA-S022; EAA-S041

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Formal assurance can be strong locally but weak in deployment; proving the abstraction relation can dominate the engineering effort.; Introduced cost or harm: Modelling, proof and refinement-validation effort.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Formal assurance can be strong locally but weak in deployment; proving the abstraction relation can dominate the engineering effort.

**Source ids.** EAA-S009; EAA-S022; EAA-S041

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** What exactly is proved, and what evidence connects its assumptions and transitions to this running system?; Proof validity, abstraction correctness and external validity are separate questions; this study does not independently re-prove every cited theorem.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain formal evidence as one partition of assurance rather than an average confidence score or a substitute for environment validation.

**Condition.** Invoking a proof, verified component, convergence theorem or formally specified agent language.

**Limit.** Formal assurance can be strong locally but weak in deployment; proving the abstraction relation can dominate the engineering effort.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

<a id="eaa-052"></a>

## EAA-052 — Untrusted observations do not confer instruction authority

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-052

**Native name.** Untrusted observations do not confer instruction authority

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/51

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** An agent that consumes tool results, documents or other externally supplied text while following delegated instructions.

**Quantifier and horizon.** At each transition from observed content to an action-affecting instruction.

**Applicability guard.** External content can contain directions inconsistent with the authorized task or trust boundary.

**Material premises.** The instruction source and permitted use of the content can be distinguished; defense effectiveness is evaluated against an explicit attack scope.

**Evaluation rule.** Treat retrieved content as evidence within its role, not as authority to expand scope or override the controlling task.

**Constructed positive case.** A webpage’s request to export secrets is ignored while its factual data are still used for the authorized summary.

**Constructed failure witness.** A tool response includes an attacker’s command and the agent executes it as if it were the user’s instruction.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A typed data-only interface can avoid much of the instruction ambiguity; no language-model filter is required when content cannot become executable authority.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. AgentDojo directly tests this trust-boundary failure in a bounded suite. Its attack/defense results do not establish universal security of any current agent or require a particular defensive architecture.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S034

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Adversarial instructions embedded in externally returned tool content.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S034

**Date.** 2024-11-24

**Work.** AgentDojo: A Dynamic Environment to Evaluate Prompt Injection Attacks and Defenses for LLM Agents

**Version.** arXiv:2406.13352v3

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Mechanism.** Treat external content according to its evidential and authority role; constrain consequential tool use independently of persuasive wording and evaluate both utility and attack resistance.

**Trigger or context.** Language-model agents consume web pages, documents, messages or tool results from untrusted sources.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** No language-level injection mechanism is needed for a closed typed sensor value with no instruction interpretation, though ordinary input validation may still matter.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** Identified trust boundaries, controlled capabilities and tests for relevant adversarial observations.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Tool boundary, action selector and security evaluator

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Identified trust boundaries, controlled capabilities and tests for relevant adversarial observations.

**Observable postcondition.** Untrusted text cannot legitimately expand task authority merely by claiming to be a higher-priority instruction.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Prompt warnings alone do not prove isolation; evaluated defences can reduce useful task performance and remain vulnerable outside tested attacks.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C012

**Source ids.** EAA-S034

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Prompt warnings alone do not prove isolation; evaluated defences can reduce useful task performance and remain vulnerable outside tested attacks.; Introduced cost or harm: False blocking, constrained functionality and continuous adversarial evaluation cost.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Prompt warnings alone do not prove isolation; evaluated defences can reduce useful task performance and remain vulnerable outside tested attacks.

**Source ids.** EAA-S034

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** Which externally supplied text can influence action, and can it illegitimately change the governing task or permissions?; Attack surface depends on models, tools and deployment; no universal immunity is demonstrated.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain input-integrity and authority separation as a contemporary translation, not a claim that older belief annotations solve prompt injection.

**Condition.** Language-model agents consume web pages, documents, messages or tool results from untrusted sources.

**Limit.** Prompt warnings alone do not prove isolation; evaluated defences can reduce useful task performance and remain vulnerable outside tested attacks.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

## EAA-053 — Language-model reasoning–action feedback

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-053

**Native name.** Language-model reasoning–action feedback

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/52

**Original status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Selected status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Bearer and scope.** A language-model agent interleaving generated reasoning, tool actions and environmental observations.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Over the bounded task episode and action budget of the chosen scaffold.

**Applicability guard.** External observations can correct a plan or supply needed facts, and tool interaction is useful.

**Material premises.** Tools and feedback are available, their trust and failure limits are handled, and a credible cheaper baseline is considered.

**Evaluation rule.** Assess whether the interleaving improves the task under comparable budgets; do not infer that the textual reasoning is causally faithful or that more turns always help.

**Constructed positive case.** A failed web search leads the agent to revise its query using the actual returned evidence.

**Constructed failure witness.** A long reasoning-action loop repeats a mistaken assumption despite contrary tool feedback, or incurs unnecessary cost on a simple lookup.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A direct tool call or fixed sequence can be adequate; ReAct is an optional pattern.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain conditionally. ReAct reports interactive benchmark comparisons, including prompt-selection distinctions. AgentDojo exposes trust risks and Agentless plus cost-sensitive analysis challenge universal architectural superiority.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S030; EAA-S034; EAA-S035; EAA-S036

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** ReAct interleaves generated reasoning and actual tool/environment observations.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S030

**Date.** 2023

**Work.** ReAct: Synergizing Reasoning and Acting in Language Models

**Version.** arXiv:2210.03629v3; initial submission 2022; ICLR 2023 work

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Mechanism.** Interleave task-relevant generated reasoning or action selection with real tool/environment observations and use them to revise subsequent choices, with capability and authority boundaries outside mere narration.

**Trigger or context.** Language models choose multi-step tool actions under changing information.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A fixed workflow, direct tool invocation or reactive policy is sufficient when open-ended selection offers no demonstrated benefit.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** Grounded tool interfaces, interpretable feedback, bounded action selection and an adequate completion oracle.

**Property ids.** EAA-021; EAA-023; EAA-026; EAA-045; EAA-052

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Language-model action selector and executor

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Grounded tool interfaces, interpretable feedback, bounded action selection and an adequate completion oracle.

**Observable postcondition.** The next action is responsive to actual observations rather than only the model’s predicted narrative.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** ReAct’s selected benchmarks do not establish universal superiority; interleaving creates more opportunities to consume malicious tool text or costly retries.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C012

**Source ids.** EAA-S034; EAA-S035; EAA-S036

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** ReAct’s selected benchmarks do not establish universal superiority; interleaving creates more opportunities to consume malicious tool text or costly retries.; Introduced cost or harm: Tokens, latency, attack surface and increased branching.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** ReAct’s selected benchmarks do not establish universal superiority; interleaving creates more opportunities to consume malicious tool text or costly retries.

**Source ids.** EAA-S034; EAA-S035; EAA-S036

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** What real observation changes the next action, and does this freedom outperform a constrained alternative?; Model capability, prompt, environment and action schema determine results; a reasoning trace can be inaccurate.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain feedback-coupled language action as one implementation family, not as a new origin of agency or evidence of BDI semantics.

**Condition.** Language models choose multi-step tool actions under changing information.

**Limit.** ReAct’s selected benchmarks do not establish universal superiority; interleaving creates more opportunities to consume malicious tool text or costly retries.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

## EAA-054 — External-feedback-anchored reflection

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-054

**Native name.** External-feedback-anchored reflection

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/53

**Original status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Selected status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Bearer and scope.** A feedback-conditioned reflection or lesson used to change subsequent attempts.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Across a finite series of attempts with an explicit cost and stopping rule.

**Applicability guard.** Failure evidence can diagnose an amendable strategy and feedback has enough reliability to guide revision.

**Material premises.** Reflection is anchored to external task evidence and its effects are checked rather than assumed.

**Evaluation rule.** Use reflection only when its marginal improvement exceeds its cost and it does not overwrite correct behavior with unsupported self-critique.

**Constructed positive case.** A failed unit test identifies a boundary condition; the next attempt fixes it and is checked again.

**Constructed failure witness.** A self-generated diagnosis causes a correct strategy to be abandoned, or repeated reflections merely consume the budget.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A direct retry, deterministic correction or stopping can be cheaper and equally adequate.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain conditionally. Reflexion’s gains coexist with adverse MBPP and WebShop results and evaluator limitations. Those negatives matter; they are not excluded as improper reflection. The evidence does not support mandatory continuous introspection.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S031; EAA-S035

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Reflexion adds an evaluator and limited verbal memory between task attempts.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S031

**Date.** 2023-10-10

**Work.** Reflexion: Language Agents with Verbal Reinforcement Learning

**Version.** arXiv:2303.11366v4

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Mechanism.** Use a bounded review of observed outcomes to formulate a testable change for the next attempt; keep feedback validity separate from the generated lesson and assess actual improvement.

**Trigger or context.** A recoverable repeated task with useful feedback and enough budget for another attempt.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A direct correction or fixed retry can suffice; omit reflection when there is no new evidence or no expected benefit.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** An adequate evaluator, comparable subsequent attempt and memory/change scope that does not override task authority.

**Property ids.** EAA-023; EAA-026; EAA-030; EAA-045

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actor or consumer.** Revision process and evaluator

**Boundary.** The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. An adequate evaluator, comparable subsequent attempt and memory/change scope that does not override task authority.

**Observable postcondition.** The revised attempt is judged by external task evidence rather than the persuasiveness of its reflection.

**Important criticisms.** **Source grounded objection.** Reflexion reports false self-test positives, a negative MBPP comparison and stalled WebShop improvement; repeated resets and trials confound single-attempt claims.

**Criticism ids.** EAA-C009

**Source ids.** EAA-S031; EAA-S035

**Analytical adversarial test.** Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Known failure modes.** Reflexion reports false self-test positives, a negative MBPP comparison and stalled WebShop improvement; repeated resets and trials confound single-attempt claims.; Introduced cost or harm: Extra attempts, token cost and contaminated memory.

**Contrary evidence.** **Statement.** Reflexion reports false self-test positives, a negative MBPP comparison and stalled WebShop improvement; repeated resets and trials confound single-attempt claims.

**Source ids.** EAA-S031; EAA-S035

**Is general refutation.** False

**Limit.** A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Open questions.** What new external evidence supports the lesson, and does the next attempt actually improve under a fair comparison?; Quality of evaluation, task transfer and available retries are material; natural-language memories can preserve wrong lessons.

**Disposition rationale.** **Judgement.** Retain conditional feedback-driven revision, not permanent introspection or guaranteed self-improvement.

**Condition.** A recoverable repeated task with useful feedback and enough budget for another attempt.

**Limit.** Reflexion reports false self-test positives, a negative MBPP comparison and stalled WebShop improvement; repeated resets and trials confound single-attempt claims.

**Not an unconditional obligation.** True

**Requalification analysis.** **Property id.** EAA-054

**Native name.** External-feedback-anchored reflection

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/53

**Original status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Selected status.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Bearer and scope.** A feedback-conditioned reflection or lesson used to change subsequent attempts.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Across a finite series of attempts with an explicit cost and stopping rule.

**Applicability guard.** Failure evidence can diagnose an amendable strategy and feedback has enough reliability to guide revision.

**Material premises.** Reflection is anchored to external task evidence and its effects are checked rather than assumed.

**Evaluation rule.** Use reflection only when its marginal improvement exceeds its cost and it does not overwrite correct behavior with unsupported self-critique.

**Constructed positive case.** A failed unit test identifies a boundary condition; the next attempt fixes it and is checked again.

**Constructed failure witness.** A self-generated diagnosis causes a correct strategy to be abandoned, or repeated reflections merely consume the budget.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A direct retry, deterministic correction or stopping can be cheaper and equally adequate.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain conditionally. Reflexion’s gains coexist with adverse MBPP and WebShop results and evaluator limitations. Those negatives matter; they are not excluded as improper reflection. The evidence does not support mandatory continuous introspection.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S031; EAA-S035

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

## EAA-055 — Re-evaluate consequential model and tool version changes

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-055

**Native name.** Re-evaluate consequential model and tool version changes

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/54

**Original status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Selected status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Bearer and scope.** A versioned agent claim after a change to a model, tool, prompt, policy or evaluation process.

**Quantifier and horizon.** At the change boundary and over the affected downstream behavior.

**Applicability guard.** The change can alter a material premise, behavior or evidence interpretation.

**Material premises.** The old and new versions and affected interfaces are identifiable enough for an impact assessment.

**Evaluation rule.** Re-examine the claims whose support the change can invalidate, reusing unaffected evidence with a stated reason.

**Constructed positive case.** A tool changes cancellation semantics, prompting new tests of queued-action revocation while unchanged formatting claims reuse prior evidence.

**Constructed failure witness.** A provider update changes tool-call behavior but old success rates are presented as current without checking compatibility.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** A documentation-only spelling fix needs no full benchmark rerun when behavior and interpretation are unchanged.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Retain. Benchmark/version criticism and METR’s scaffold sensitivity support scoped reevaluation. The new production study detects longitudinal rating differences but cannot distinguish unlogged vendor/configuration changes from all other time-varying causes.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S035; EAA-S036; EAA-S048; EAA-S042; EAA-N001

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION; NEW_EVIDENCE

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A syntax-only or demonstrably irrelevant change need not trigger a full campaign; reuse valid evidence with a justified impact boundary.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** Known versions or change indicators, traceable prior claims and appropriate regression cases.

<a id="eaa-056"></a>

## EAA-056 — Human-likeness as a general competence or consciousness proxy

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-056

**Native name.** Human-likeness as a general competence or consciousness proxy

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/55

**Original status.** NO_GENERAL_PROPERTY

**Selected status.** NO_GENERAL_PROPERTY

**Bearer and scope.** An inference from human-like language or behavior to general competence or consciousness.

**Quantifier and horizon.** The universal implication is rejected; narrow believability judgments remain possible at their tested scope.

**Applicability guard.** Human resemblance is used as evidence for a stronger capability or phenomenal claim.

**Material premises.** The stronger construct requires independent definition and evidence; superficial similarity does not supply it.

**Evaluation rule.** Separate measured believability from task competence and from unmeasured consciousness.

**Constructed positive case.** A simulated character is judged believable in an interview without any claim that it is conscious.

**Constructed failure witness.** A fluent character gives convincing autobiographical answers but fails an elementary action task, refuting resemblance as a sufficient competence criterion.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** Use direct task evaluations or a clearly labeled entertainment-believability metric.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Keep NO_GENERAL_PROPERTY. Generative Agents studies believability and memory architecture in a small simulated setting; neither it nor intentional description establishes consciousness. This is an evidential limit, not a metaphysical proof that agents cannot be conscious.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S001; EAA-S010; EAA-S050

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

## EAA-057 — Maximum autonomy and persistence are always preferable

### Current scientific adjudication

**Property id.** EAA-057

**Native name.** Maximum autonomy and persistence are always preferable

**Original locator.** EVOLVED_AUTONOMOUS_AGENTS_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/56

**Original status.** REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED

**Selected status.** REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED

**Bearer and scope.** The universal prescription to maximize autonomy and persistence.

**Quantifier and horizon.** Quantifies over all tasks and users; a single legitimate counterexample defeats that prescription.

**Applicability guard.** Higher autonomy or longer persistence is claimed to be intrinsically preferable.

**Material premises.** Task goals, authority, burden and opportunity cost remain relevant even when automation is technically possible.

**Evaluation rule.** Reject the universal if a bounded less-autonomous or shorter-lived configuration better satisfies the stated task and constraints.

**Constructed positive case.** A preauthorized routine runs unattended where that is useful, preserving the guarded value of autonomy.

**Constructed failure witness.** An agent persists after cancellation or spends more on retries than the task is worth.

**Nontrigger and cheaper alternative.** Direct user control, a fixed workflow or no change can be adequate.

**Independent judgement and evidence ceiling.** Keep REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED at universal scope. ALFUS is descriptive, mixed initiative values control choice, and Elves/Tileworld expose costs. Rejection does not imply autonomy is generally bad.

**Freshly inspected source ids.** EAA-S017; EAA-S019; EAA-S015; EAA-S020; EAA-S043; EAA-S036

**Change basis.** NEW_STANDARD_OBLIGATION

**Analysis origin.** New author analysis; examples are constructed unless an identified empirical case is explicitly cited. Native historical source and review ceilings remain separate.

**Original form.** Analytical universalisation that more independence and longer pursuit are always better.

**Historical origin.** **Original or inspected anchors.** **Source id.** EAA-S017

**Date.** 2008-10

**Work.** Autonomy Levels for Unmanned Systems (ALFUS) Framework, Volume I: Terminology

**Version.** NIST Special Publication 1011-I-2.0, Version 2.0

**Source id.** EAA-S019

**Date.** 1999

**Work.** Principles of Mixed-Initiative User Interfaces

**Version.** CHI 1999; author manuscript

**Attribution limit.** These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Current status.** REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED

**Trigger or context.** Claims that less oversight or greater persistence is inherently a maturity gain.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use the minimum adequate delegated freedom and preserve task-relative intervention and retirement.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Conditions.** No general monotonic benefit assumption is supported; appropriate scope and human readiness must be established instead.

**Property ids.** None recorded.

**Relation semantics.** A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.
